### SEC EDGAR + FMP PE History Fix — follow-ups (updated 2026-07-29, see DONE entries same date — #4 for the XBRL rollout, #8 for the pre-2009 legacy-filing extension)

**PE-01a: Dev universe rollout (XBRL-only)** — ✅ **DONE 2026-07-29** (see DONE entry #4 same date for full detail). All 193 dev `conviction_store` records recalculated via `scripts/update_conviction_fundamentals.py --mode full --include-existing-records --pe-history-report`, zero fetch errors. 32 tickers now have ≥10 years of real P/E history (were ≤2y before), incl. MSFT/ADBE/GS/JPM/MCD/NKE/ORCL/PG/UPS at ~17y (206 monthly points), AAPL 190, NVDA 178, PYPL 133. PYPL's original bug is fixed: `pe_hist_percentile` −3.0 → 0.0, `valuation_tax` −4.0 → −1.0. **One known remaining edge case: `SONY`** still shows the old `pe_percentile_20y=100.0`/`pe_hist_percentile=-3.0` bug pattern — `is_us_ticker("SONY")` misclassifies it as US (bare ticker, no suffix) but Sony is a Japanese filer with no usable SEC EDGAR US-GAAP EPS data, so it silently falls through to the old thin yfinance path. Needs either an `is_us_ticker()` exception list for known bare-ticker foreign filers, or routing SONY through `scripts/set_manual_pe_history.py` instead. **Superseded in practice by PE-04 below** — the legacy extension changes what a fresh `full_recalculation` run produces for the tickers this rollout already touched.

**PE-04: Dev universe re-rollout to pick up the pre-2009 legacy-filing extension** — ✅ **DONE 2026-07-29** (see DONE entry #9 same date for full detail, incl. a stale-cache bug caught and fixed mid-rollout). Final corrected result: 193/193 updated, 0 errors, **18 tickers** now `pe_history_meta.source="sec_edgar+legacy"` with 25-32y real coverage (AAPL, ADBE, BAC, CSCO, CVS, GS, JPM, MAR, MCD, MSFT, MU, NKE, NVDA, PFE, PG, SBUX, UPS, WMT) — up from 0 before this rollout. `sufficient_20y_count` universe-wide: 0→18 (13.5%), `insufficient_20y_count`: 133→115 (86.5%). `pe_percentile_20y` now populates for the first time for all 18 (e.g. AAPL 100.0, WMT 97.22, NVDA 95.39). **Important operational lesson recorded**: the first pass of this rollout silently no-op'd the legacy extension for 42 tickers because their on-disk `{TICKER}_sec.json` XBRL cache (80-day TTL) pre-dated this code change and the cache-hit path returns before the legacy-extension gate is ever reached — any future PE-history *logic* change requires purging `conviction_store/pe_history_cache/*_sec.json` first, not just re-running the script.

**PE-01b: Prod universe rollout (now bundles the XBRL rollout, the legacy-filing extension, AND the Canada MJDS/IFRS extension)** — **NOT STARTED, and NOT agent-executable** — running `update_conviction_fundamentals.py` against `/home/ubuntu/uiv2/prod/MindWealth_UI/conviction_store/` is a write to prod runtime data, explicitly forbidden for an agent per the repo rules (prod clone is pull/deploy-only). A human/ops action must run the same command directly on the prod host against the prod checkout, or explicitly invoke the break-glass exception. Prod is still on the pre-fix data (171/193 records stale as of the 2026-07-29 status check) until this runs.
- **Runbook for whoever runs it**: merge `chatbot-dev`→`chatbot-prod` first (brings in both the 2026-07-24 SEC EDGAR pivot and the 2026-07-29 legacy-filing extension in one go), then:
  ```bash
  cd /home/ubuntu/uiv2/prod/MindWealth_UI
  rm -f conviction_store/pe_history_cache/*_sec.json   # only needed if prod has ever run a PE rollout before this merge — see PE-04's cache-staleness lesson above; safe/fast if prod has never run one (dir will simply be empty)
  python scripts/update_conviction_fundamentals.py --mode full --include-existing-records --pe-history-report
  ```
  Sanity-check afterward: PYPL moves off `pe_percentile_20y=100.0`/`valuation_tax=-4.0` (same shift verified on dev in entry #4); `sufficient_20y_count` in the `--pe-history-report` output should land near dev's 18/193 (13.5%), not 0.
- **Files**: `src/conviction_engine/pe_history_sec.py`, `src/conviction_engine/pe_history_sec_legacy.py`, `src/conviction_engine/pe_history_core.py`, `scripts/update_conviction_fundamentals.py`, `conviction_store/*.json` (dev fully done; prod pending both rollouts, not git-tracked)

**PE-05: TRI.TO/BN.TO FX-aware extension (deferred from the 2026-07-29 Canada MJDS work, see DONE entry #10)**
- Real IFRS EPS data exists on SEC for these two (USD-denominated, via the same 40-F/6-K mechanism as TD/RY/BNS/CNQ) but they trade in CAD on the TSX — pairing USD EPS with a CAD price needs a CAD/USD FX time series that doesn't exist anywhere in this codebase yet. Deliberately shipped as "not extended" rather than risk a silently-wrong P/E ratio.
- **Next step if pursued**: source a free daily CAD/USD rate history (e.g. `yfinance`'s `CAD=X` ticker) and either convert the USD EPS to CAD or the CAD price to USD before computing the ratio — needs its own validation pass (spot-check against a known-correct P/E for at least one date) before trusting it.
- **Files**: `src/conviction_engine/pe_history_sec.py` (`FOREIGN_PRIVATE_ISSUER_ALIASES`)

**PE-06: India NSE/BSE structured PE-history source — decision needed before building (see DONE entry #10 for full research)**
- Original plan (build a clean SEC-EDGAR-style official API fetcher) doesn't hold up: NSE's real XBRL results sit behind Akamai bot-protection needing a manually-refreshed browser cookie (same fragile risk class that already ruled out Macrotrends), and even if bypassed, the current XBRL format only covers 2024+ (≈2 years, not 20). BSE's structured data is exclusively paid via Deutsche Börse.
- **Blocked on a user decision** between: (a) still build the cookie-bypass NSE fetcher accepting the maintenance/ToS risk and the 2024+-only depth ceiling, (b) build a PDF-parsing extractor against `nsearchives.nseindia.com`'s public annual-report archive (~2011+ depth, but bespoke per-filer parsing, no structured schedule — closer in effort to a second legacy-filing extractor than an API integration), (c) route the 14 `.NS` tickers through manual entry (PE-03) instead, or (d) drop India for now.
- **Files**: none yet (research only, no code written for this item)

**PE-07: New Zealand NZX PE-history source — paid-tier decision needed (see DONE entry #10's predecessor research, 2026-07-29 country-sources pass)**
- A third-party API (NZXplorer) has real structured NZX data back to ~2010, but its free tier's terms of service explicitly restrict use to "personal projects, research, prototyping" and explicitly ban bulk extraction/redistribution — using it in MindWealth's production engine would need their paid Pro tier (~$29/mo) or a commercial license, not the free tier as-is. NZX's own official data product (Company Research Centre) is a separate paid subscription.
- **Blocked on a user decision**: pay for NZXplorer Pro / contact NZX directly for a commercial quote, or route the 17 `.NZ` tickers through manual entry (PE-03) instead.
- **Files**: none yet (research only, no code written for this item)

### Data & Pipeline Integrity Audit — 2026-06-06
_Findings from web-verified cross-check of nightly report variables and combo classifications._

#### 🔴 Priority 1 — Fix Required

~~**T-01: Fed Cycle persists as "CUTTING_LATE" during 5-month Fed pause with hike risk**~~ → **FIXED 2026-07-22** — see DONE section.
- **What**: `fed_cycle_at_date()` in `fed_cycle.py` carries forward the last known cycle label (`CUTTING_LATE`) during a PAUSE via the `series.iloc[-1]` fallback (lines 187-190). The Fed has been on hold at 3.50–3.75% since at least January 2026 (FOMC meetings Jan 28, Mar 18, Apr 29 all held). The Jun 5 jobs report (172K, above consensus) now has markets pricing 42.7% probability of a Dec 2026 rate HIKE. Reporting `CUTTING_LATE` when the Fed is paused and hike-risk is rising mis-frames the narrative and regime analysis.
- **Fix needed**: Introduce a `PAUSING` label that activates after N consecutive weeks of PAUSE following a cutting cycle, OR add a `hike_risk_threshold` (e.g. CME FedWatch >30%) override. At minimum, add `PAUSING_POST_CUT` as a distinct state after `cycle_early_months` of pause.
- **Files**: `src/macro_intelligence/engine/fed_cycle.py`, `macro_intelligence/CONFIG.yaml`

~~**T-02: Nightly job does not auto-refresh CFTC TFF ZIP on Fridays — data goes stale by one week**~~ → **FIXED 2026-06-07** — see DONE section.

~~**T-03: `_combo_c_weeks()` will show perpetual Week 1 when Combo C re-activates**~~ → **FIXED 2026-06-07** — episode anchor pattern applied (see DONE #15).

~~**T-03: VIX single-day spike magnitude not detected — tier stays NORMAL despite +40% jump**~~ → **FIXED 2026-07-22** — see DONE section.
- **What**: On Jun 5, 2026, VIX jumped 39.68% (15.40 → 21.51) driven by Broadcom guidance miss and strong jobs report (chip stocks lost $1.3T market cap; S&P 500 −2.63%). The system classifies VIX as NORMAL because the absolute level (21.51) is below the RARE threshold (25.0 abs_level). No mechanism exists to detect single-day spike magnitude as a stress signal. A 40% one-day spike is historically significant and should escalate tier.
- **Fix needed**: Add `single_day_pct_change` threshold to VIX tier logic in `evaluate_variable_tier()` (e.g. daily change ≥ 25% → RARE, ≥ 40% → EXTREME regardless of abs level). Requires pulling prior-day VIX close alongside current.
- **Files**: `src/macro_intelligence/engine/percentiles.py`, `macro_intelligence/CONFIG.yaml`

---

#### 🟡 Priority 2 — Investigate / Improve

**T-03: CAPE value inconsistency between sources (~1.3pt gap)**
- **What**: Report shows CAPE = 42.70. Multpl (Shiller's official data): 42.84 (Jun 2, 2026). GuruFocus: 41.44 (Jun 2026). The 1.3-point GuruFocus gap is meaningful at the EXTREME classification threshold (32). If the system scrapes from a third source, it may drift from Shiller's canonical series.
- **Fix needed**: Confirm which URL `cape_scrape.py` fetches from; standardise to `multpl.com/shiller-pe` (Shiller's official distributor). Add a sanity-check log warning if scraped CAPE deviates >1.0 from prior week's value.
- **Files**: `src/macro_intelligence/data/cape_scrape.py`

**T-04: Combo B WATCH triggered by only 1 of 3 required conditions (CFTC only)**
- **What**: Combo B (Capitulation) requires VIX ≥ 25, HY ≥ 400bps, AND CFTC ≤ 15th pctile. Current: VIX = 21.51 (❌), HY = 274bps (❌), CFTC = 5th pctile (✅). Only 1 of 3 legs met, yet the report displays B as WATCH with "BULLISH" direction. Per architecture §4.6, 1+ legs = WATCH, but this single-leg WATCH may mislead readers into thinking capitulation is forming.
- **Fix needed**: In the combo status table, add a "Legs Met" sub-column (e.g. "1/3") so readers understand how far B is from activation. Alternatively, raise the WATCH floor to ≥ 2/3 conditions.
- **Files**: `src/macro_intelligence/output/briefing_renderer.py`, `src/macro_intelligence/engine/combo_detector.py`

**T-05: Combo F Week 1 activation on a down-2.63% day needs reclaim validation**
- **What**: Combo F (Recovery) fired as ACTIVE Week 1. The trigger requires SPX weekly close to be 3%+ above the 50-week moving average. SPX closed Jun 5 at 7,384.67 — below its 50-DAY MA (7,561.51). Cannot independently confirm the 50-WEEK MA level from web, but the 9-week winning streak that just ended and prior lows make it plausible SPX is above the 50wma. However, the combo firing on the same week the winning streak broke (−2.63% selloff) needs a review of whether the Friday close was the trigger date.
- **Fix needed**: Add a log line in `combo_detector.py` that prints SPX close vs 50wma value and % above when Combo F fires, so the trigger can be audited in the nightly log. Also ensure the detection uses the weekly close (not an intraday print) for the 3% reclaim check.
- **Files**: `src/macro_intelligence/engine/combo_detector.py`, `jobs/nightly_run.py`

#### ⚪ Priority 3 — Data Gaps (Cannot Web-Verify)

**T-06: WALCL, CNH, WTI (4wk change), VXTS, CFTC raw value, CPI surprise cannot be independently web-verified**
- **What**: These 6 variables pull from FRED/Yahoo/CFTC data with weekly update cycles and lag. The values look internally consistent but cannot be confirmed via public web search without API access.
- **Fix needed**: Add a weekly data validation script that fetches each variable from its primary source and compares against the stored DB value, logging any deviation > configured threshold. Alert to Slack/email if any variable is stale (> N days old) or deviates > X% from live feed.
- **Files**: `scripts/export_data_validation.py` (extend), `src/macro_intelligence/data/pull_all.py`

1. **New skill `do-task` — task description → root-cause fix pipeline** — SUCCESSFUL
   - Summary: Added `.claude/skills/do-task/SKILL.md`, a project-scoped, explicitly-invoked skill (`disable-model-invocation: true`) that takes a pasted task description (bug report, feature ask, chat/email paste, todo row) and drives it end to end in 7 phases: (0) restate the task in a fixed table so the symptom wording is not paraphrased away, (1) gather context — codebase call chain with `file:line`, the three job-status docs, then the `gmail-filtered` and `mindwealth-todos` MCP servers only when the codebase does not answer it, (2) reproduce the visible issue with a pasted command + output before any fix, (3) root cause — "earliest point where correct input became wrong output", with a symptom-vs-root-cause table (null check at render vs upstream silent fetch failure, UI clamp vs wrong scaling factor, retry vs cached expired token, regenerate CSV vs loader cursor never advancing) plus required "why it surfaced now" and "blast radius" statements, (4) fix at the root layer with the blast radius covered or explicitly listed, (5) verify by rerunning the Phase 2 repro, (6) clarifications for Rohit sir split blocking vs non-blocking (blocking = business logic: thresholds, formulas, regime rules), (7) the repo's mandatory logging protocol. Ends with a fixed final-response format (Task / Symptom / Root cause / Fix / Verified / Left out / Ask Rohit / Logged).

2. **Claude v3 Addendum Q2–Q5 — answer the five questions against live code and fix the Q4 divergence (short-signal alpha benchmark)** — SUCCESSFUL
   - Summary: Answered Rohit sir's five open questions from the 11 Aug "Claude v3 Addendum" mail directly against the live code in both repos, and corrected the one place the code diverged from the spec. **Q2 (composite scalar vs sub-scores):** `compute_composite_score()` in `MindWealth/helper_functions/claude_lateness_metrics.py:850` returns a single float; `er_score` / `alpha_score` / `sharpe_score` exist only as internal formula terms (`c1`/`c2`/`c3`/`c4`) and are never emitted as payload fields; `cagr_score` does not exist anywhere. **Q3 (3-component v3 vs 4-component v4):** the MasterSpec section C text says 3 components with CAGR_diff as a diagnostic only, `MindWealth_Composite_v4_Calibrated` says 4 — code and the live prompt both implement **v4** (C1 E[R] 40 + C2 alpha 25 + C3 Sharpe 20 + C4 CAGR_diff 10). **Q4 (short-signal alpha benchmark): DIVERGED — now fixed.** Live code set `signal_alpha = er` for shorts with no benchmark subtracted at all (comment: "Conservative: no drift credit for short signals"), which matched neither the addendum's `random_window_return_short = −(B&H_CAGR/252 × avg_hold_days)` nor the older IRX position. `compute_random_window_return()` now takes `direction` and returns the mirrored (negated) baseline for shorts, so `signal_alpha = er − random_window_return` holds for both directions, unfloored, identical formula across every asset class. **Q4b (stray 0/IRX use for shorts):** pipeline audit across both repos found **no violations** outside the four allowed uses — Sharpe risk-free rate and short-rebate accrual (`src/portfolio_nav/portfolio_sharpe_analysis.py`), the rf reporting convention, and the Gate A2d/CAGR_diff display wording `"cash (IRX)" if is_short else "B&H"` (`claude_lateness_metrics.py:1128` and its mirror `api/services/signal_enrichment_service.py:177`), which is a tooltip string and not signal-alpha math. A stale comment claiming `bt_bh_cagr` was "reserved for future IRX-specific short benchmarks" was corrected in place. **Q5 (which Gate A2 is live):** **both, simultaneously** — `MindWealth/constant.py` carries the base-prompt Gate A2 (historical Win Rate ≥ 70% as a single test) *and* A2a (E[R] floor), A2b (fwd WR ≥ 60), A2c (conviction), A2d (signal-alpha nuanced) and A2e (R:R ≥ 1.0) as separate gates.
   - Verification: repro script showed the bug (Short, B&H 7%, 18d hold → `signal_alpha = 3.125` = `er`, unchanged whether B&H drift was +7% or −4%); after the fix the same rows give `signal_alpha = 3.625` for B&H +7% (baseline −0.5, drift credit) and `2.8393` for B&H −4% (baseline +0.2857, bar raised), Long unchanged at `2.625`. New regression test `test_short_signal_alpha_uses_random_short_benchmark` added; `pytest tests/test_claude_lateness_metrics.py tests/test_signals_masterspec_g3.py` → **39 passed**. `tests/test_cross_function_exit.py` cannot be collected in this environment (pre-existing `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'joblib'` via `data.py`, unrelated to this change).
   - Files changed: `MindWealth/helper_functions/claude_lateness_metrics.py` (`compute_random_window_return` direction-aware, signal-alpha branch collapsed, stale IRX comment), `MindWealth/constant.py` (prompt field definitions for `random_window_return` / `signal_alpha_per_trade`, and the `Signal Alpha Per Trade [%]` glossary tooltip), `MindWealth/tests/test_signals_masterspec_g3.py` (new test).
   - Prod impact: **yes** — short-signal `signal_alpha_per_trade`, `signal_alpha_annualized` and `composite_score` values change, and the Claude prompt text changes. No UI-repo code change was needed (`api/services/signal_enrichment_service.py` imports `enrich_signal_dict` from the core repo and otherwise reads the pre-computed CSV column). Logged in `dev_to_prod_migration_todos.md`.

4. **Claude v3 Addendum §1 — hard rule in the live signals prompt: never recompute the pre-computed payload fields** — SUCCESSFUL
   - Task (verbatim from the todo row): "Addendum section 1: add the hard rule that Claude must not recompute composite_score, tier, alpha_interpretation, exit_fired, conviction_bq_score or conviction_fs_class, and must use the fixed missing-field wording instead of estimating a substitute."
   - Live prompt located: `MindWealth/constant.py` `GOOD_SIGNAL_QUERY` (lines 316-793 before this change) — the only prompt that reaches Claude for the nightly signals report (`send_email.py:2374`, `:2432`, `:3736`, and `regen_claude_report.py:120`). `GOOD_SIGNAL_QUERY3_OLD` / `_old` / `_v1` in the same file are dead. The UI repo's chatbot prompt (`prompts/chatbot_system.txt`, `prompts/engine.py`) never mentions these fields, so nothing there needed changing.
   - **Prompt change** — new section **2.2a HARD RULE — PRE-COMPUTED FIELDS, DO NOT RECOMPUTE**, placed immediately after 2.2 "Precompute-first" and declared to win over any conflicting instruction later in the prompt. It names the six fields, forbids recalculation from raw win-rate / CAGR / return / conviction fields and forbids building any private scoring formula, records that `er_score` / `alpha_score` / `sharpe_score` are upstream formula variables and not payload fields, and states there is no `cagr_score`. Missing-field handling uses Rohit sir's exact wording: *"Not available in model output — {field} missing for {row}; cannot confirm tier without it — placed in Tier C pending data."* Reinforced in three more places: a new bullet in the section 0 "IMPORTANT — DO NOT" list; the Section 9H Composite v4 formula re-labelled **"REFERENCE ONLY — DO NOT COMPUTE (see 2.2a)"**; and the `surface_json` instructions plus the four field definitions (`composite_score`, `tier`, `alpha_interpretation`, `exit_fired`) marked "Payload-provided; echo verbatim".
   - **Applicability carve-out (added after measuring the real data, not assumed).** A literal reading of §1 would demote every row without conviction data to Tier C. On the 2026-08-19 outstanding file that is **28 of 81 rows** — ETFs, indices, FX and crypto that the Conviction Engine does not cover by design (Gate A2c is equity-only), plus 9 rows `derive_asset_class` mislabels as `equity` (ACWX, EFA, IHI, XLE, MSE.PA, NIFTY_MIDCAP_100.NS). So the Tier C placement is scoped to the four model-output fields; absent conviction fields get "Conviction: NOT_APPLICABLE (not covered by Conviction Engine)" and the row continues, matching the existing Gate A2c rule ("if conviction data is NOT in payload → skip this gate"). Substituting `conviction_score` or `fs_score` for them is still forbidden.
   - **Payload gap fixed (the rule would otherwise be unsatisfiable).** `composite_score`, `tier`, `alpha_interpretation` and `exit_fired` already reach Claude — `enrich_signal_dict()` writes them into each signal dict before `json.dumps` (`send_email.py:2365`). `conviction_bq_score` and `conviction_fs_class` did **not**: `_load_conviction_lookup` (`send_email.py:145-155`) copied only `conviction_score`/`conviction_raw`/`fs_class`/`yield_trap`/`fd_direction`/`verdict`/`sizing_pct`, ignoring the overlay CSV's `bq_raw` and `coverage_incomplete` columns. After: non-null on exactly the **53** rows that have `conviction_bq_score`, `None` on all 28 carve-out rows, and `inject_python_surface_json` output contains no `NaN` literal and passes a strict JSON parse (`parse_constant` raising on non-finite). 4. **New skill `robust-test-and-prod-deploy` — formalizes entry 3's merge/deploy playbook** — SUCCESSFUL
   - Task: mirror `robust-test-and-dev-deploy` but for the `chatbot-dev` → `chatbot-prod` release path, requested right after entry 3 above was done manually.
   - `.claude/skills/robust-test-and-prod-deploy/SKILL.md` (9-step master checklist) codifies the two blockers found the hard way in entry 3, so the next release doesn't have to rediscover them: (1) a read-only pre-flight drift check — local `chatbot-prod` vs `origin/chatbot-prod`, dev-vs-prod commit/file size, prod-clone dirty-file audit, `requirements.txt` diff, concurrent-session scan — because entry 3's merge base would have been wrong (5 commits reverted) had it trusted `origin/chatbot-prod` blindly; (2) an isolated `git worktree` merge with **zero tolerance on conflicts** (stop and ask, never auto-resolve source code), because entry 3 found a live concurrent session mid-edit in the shared checkout; (3) the prod-clone stash → pull → stash-pop cycle with **timestamp-based, not convention-based** conflict resolution for cron-owned data files (`monitored_trades.json`, SSI CSVs, `aaii_sentiment.xls`), rebuilding each resolved file via `git show stash@{0}:<path>` since `checkout --ours/--theirs` doesn't populate merge stages on a stash-pop conflict, then unstaging (`git reset`) and only dropping the stash after a `<<<<<<<`/`>>>>>>>` grep confirms it's clean — prod must never carry a commit, not even mid-resolution; (4) a migration-doc "closure entry" template that states plainly what a merge did and did NOT verify (git-file merge + restart, yes; individual `[PROD-ACTION]` runtime/config steps in historical entries, core-repo prompt work, `MindwealthUI_Vue` — no) rather than blanket-marking 71 historical entries `[DONE]`.
   - New reusable script `.claude/skills/robust-test-and-prod-deploy/scripts/check-prod-drift.sh` — read-only, no writes/pushes/restarts. Ran it live: confirmed local/origin `chatbot-prod` back in sync post-entry-3, flagged an **unrelated concurrent session** on the host (a `backfill_geo_overlay.py` geo backfill loop, separate from the `superindex.py`/`positioning.py` session noted in entry 3) — exactly the class of thing step 1 exists to catch before a merge.
   - `disable-model-invocation: true` (matches `prod-pull-and-details` and `robust-test-and-dev-deploy`) — explicit invocation only, given the blast radius of a live prod push + service restart. Reuses `robust-test-and-dev-deploy`'s `git-commit-dev.sh`/`git-push-dev.sh` and `prod-pull-and-details`'s `smoke-test-apis.sh` rather than duplicating them.
   - Files changed: **new** `.claude/skills/robust-test-and-prod-deploy/{SKILL.md,reference.md,scripts/check-prod-drift.sh}`
   - Prod impact: **none** — agent tooling only, no runtime code, no API surface, no config. `dev_to_prod_migration_todos.md` not updated for this entry.

3. **SSI truth-telling fixes — no invented Layer 3 score, excluded inputs labelled, CNN crypto rows removed, regime multipliers persisted** — SUCCESSFUL
   - Summary: Fixed the defects the 2026-08-20 verification sweep found, dev only. (a) **Invented number:** the Sentiment page printed `LAYER 3 · POSITIONING +0.300` on a day the API returned `layer3.score: null` — the Nuxt mapper fell back to `ssi_level` rounded to 1dp. The API now publishes `partial_score` (the real weighted mean over surviving inputs) and the page shows `+0.863 · 25% partial z-score, layer not scored`, or nothing at all when there is no partial. (b) **Silent drops:** every component now carries `excluded_from_score` + `excluded_reason`, so the three CFTC rows read `excluded from score - expired - last print 9d old, weekly carry cap 8d` in amber instead of printing live-looking nets with no note. (c) **Two clocks:** `inputs_meta.layer3_cftc` now carries the SSI scoring verdict (`scored_in_ssi`, `excluded_from_score`, `excluded_reason`) beside the release-calendar one, and the COT badge reads **Not in SSI score** instead of **Current**. (d) **CNN F&G:** 974 `crypto_proxy` rows (2018-02-03 → 2026-07-15, 24 on real SPX sessions incl. 2026-03-12 and 2026-07-15) removed — CNN's own API has no value for any of them, so the honest state is no observation and the daily carry uses the last real print; `CRYPTO_PROXY_WINDOW_END` now stops `backfill_cnn_from_altme()` re-adding them. SSI impact measured: level 0.2850 → 0.2855, 5y pctile 65.74 → 66.22. (e) **Regime multipliers:** `m_fed`/`m_curve`/`m_val`/`m_geo`/`m_liq`/`gross_mult` are persisted per `(date, multiplier_version)` in a new `regime_multipliers` table (1,911 rows written) and read back, so a constant change can no longer rewrite history already served — Rohit sir's 6 Aug "no interface ever recomputes". (f) **NAAIM:** re-checked every free surface (embed table frozen 2026-05-13, chart 2026-05-06, page 301, WP REST newsletters only, newest Wayback snapshot 2026-05-31, `index.naaim.org/api/exposure` 404, `api.naaim.org` no DNS) — nothing recoverable, recorded in the fetcher docstring so nobody repeats the hunt; it is a product decision.
   - Deliberately **not** applied: the CFTC staleness cap itself. The flat `weekly: 8` cap measured from the Tuesday position date is what drops COT every Wed–Thu; changing it is business logic, so it is a blocking question for Rohit sir, not a silent edit.
   - Verified: `pytest tests/ -q` → **859 passed, 4 skipped, 0 failed** (307s). `vitest test/sentiment-mapper.spec.ts` → **14 passed** (4 new regression tests: partial not composite, empty tile when no partial, excluded-row label, COT badge follows SSI). `vue-tsc` errors **45 → 42** (three pre-existing errors fixed by the nullable score type, none added). `npm run build` clean. Visual check on dev `:8514` (headless Chrome, admin login) confirms all four Layer tabs.
   - Files changed: `src/sentiment_superindex/engine/superindex.py`, `src/sentiment_superindex/engine/positioning.py`, `src/sentiment_superindex/data/cnn_fear_greed.py`, `src/sentiment_superindex/data/naaim_pull.py`, `src/macro_intelligence/output/regime_feed_export.py`, `tests/test_regime_source_of_truth.py`, **new** `scripts/repair_cnn_fg_crypto_rows.py`, data `macro_intelligence/data/ssi/cnn_fear_greed.csv`, `macro_intelligence/output/positioning.json`; in `/home/ubuntu/MindwealthUI_Vue` (`ui-dev`): `server/utils/sentiment-mapper.ts`, `pages/sentiment.vue`, `types/api.ts`, `test/sentiment-mapper.spec.ts`.
   - Prod impact: **yes** — see `dev_to_prod_migration_todos.md`. Dev services restarted (`mindwealth-api-dev`, `mindwealth-ui-dev`), both active.

4. **Rohit sir's CFTC row (v2_TODOs row 42) — all six points answered, plus two data bugs found doing it** — SUCCESSFUL
   - **Bug 1, the big one:** `_parse_report_dates` (`src/macro_intelligence/data/cftc_pull.py`) parsed the TFF date column as ISO and only fell back to a mixed parse when **every** row failed. The 2006-2016 bulk file dates as `M/D/YYYY 12:00:00 AM` while the yearly files are ISO, so in the combined frame the bulk rows coerced to NaT and were dropped in silence: FM/RM started **2017-01-03** instead of 2006-06-13 and every grid ran on **483 weeks instead of 1034**. Fixed by parsing the leftovers rather than testing whether all failed. This is what Rohit sir kept hitting as "the GFC is missing" — not a definitional problem at all.
   - **Bug 2:** `scheduled_events.fred_release_ids.FOMC` was **19**, which is *H.3 Aggregate Reserves of Depository Institutions*, a weekly reserves report. Every FOMC-dated feature (pre-catalyst fragility, post-event transitions, the new event gate) was keying off Thursday reserve prints. Release 101 (*FOMC Press Release*) is no better — FRED returns publication timestamps, ~218/yr, not the eight meetings — so FOMC was removed from the FRED path with the reason recorded, and comes from the investing.com scraper into `pending_releases` (2011+). `fetch_fred_release_dates` also gained paging; it was silently truncated at FRED's 1000-row cap.
   - **Point 2 (RM explanatory power) — answered, no.** RM regression R² ≤ 0.0044, p 0.03–0.59; adding RM on top of FM makes adjusted R² *worse* at 1/2/4w and only helps at 8w (Δ +0.0034, p 0.035 — one horizon of four). Cell test: FM<10 alone = 35 episodes, 12w mean 3.39%, excess +1.09%, hit 77.1%; adding RM>55 = 21 episodes, mean 3.35%, excess +1.05%. RM halves the sample and buys nothing.
   - **Point 3 (event coincidence) — built and run.** New `src/sentiment_superindex/analysis/cftc_event_gate.py` over CPI/FOMC/NFP releases, windows ±1/±3/±7 days, 3-episode floor. Gating does not improve SQUEEZE. It does surface the opposite finding on the other flag: the **old** LIQ EXIT cut (RM<30 and FM>60) is genuinely bearish around events — 8 episodes, 12w mean −3.00%, excess −5.31%, hit 37.5% — while the FM≥80-only placeholder is far weaker (excess −1.58%). Flagged back to Rohit sir as a reason to keep RM on that flag only.
   - **Point 4 (2003 rebuild) — built, and measured before being used.** `fetch_cftc_legacy_noncommercial_net()` pulls legacy COT `deacot2003-2010`, 417 weekly prints from 2003-01-07. Overlap vs TFF (238 weeks): level corr **0.64**, change corr 0.57, percentile corr **0.54**, mean absolute percentile difference **23 points**, and only 22 of 47 FM<10 extreme weeks agree (jaccard 0.47). Ships as labelled context (`legacy_noncommercial`), **not** stitched into the grids. The false "GFC excluded Sep 2008 – May 2009" boilerplate in `cftc_grid_v2.py` was corrected — those episodes are in the grids; the real caveat is a partial ~115-week lookback in 2008.
   - **Point 1 (recommendation):** SQUEEZE at **FM < 10th percentile, no RM leg**. FM<5 alone is worse (21 episodes, mean 2.27%, excess −0.04%); FM<5 with RM>55 is 6 episodes.
   - **Point 5 (placeholders) — shipped.** `CONFIG.yaml positioning_patterns` now `squeeze {fm_pctile_max: 10}` and `liquidity_exit {fm_pctile_min: 80}`; `_detect_positioning_pattern` treats an absent leg as "not part of the rule" instead of defaulting it back; `pattern_rules()` is published in the API so the page describes the live cut (the flag rows had hardcoded "FM <20th · RM >45th pct"). Fire counts on the full sample: squeeze 169 weeks / 35 episodes (was 181 / 37), liquidity exit 214 weeks / 36 episodes (was 162 / 40).
   - **Point 6 + the PS:** report written as markdown **and PDF** (`docs/ssi_validation/_generated/cftc_six_point_answers_20260820.md`, `docs/ssi_validation/pdf/cftc_six_point_answers_20260820.pdf`) precisely because he cannot open the Drive file, the Sheet, or the private docs repo — the PDF is the attachment.
   - Verified: `pytest -k "cftc or macro_calendar or regime_source or event"` → **61 passed**; `vitest` → **53 passed** across 5 files (2 new suites); `vue-tsc` **42 errors**, unchanged; `npm run build` clean; dev `:8514` shows `FM <10th pct (unvalidated threshold)` and `FM ≥80th pct (unvalidated threshold)` under the two flag rows.
   - Files changed: `src/macro_intelligence/data/cftc_pull.py`, `src/macro_intelligence/data/macro_calendar.py`, `macro_intelligence/CONFIG.yaml`, `src/sentiment_superindex/analysis/cftc_grid_v2.py`, `src/sentiment_superindex/data/cftc_patterns.py`, `src/sentiment_superindex/engine/positioning.py`, `tests/test_cftc_patterns.py`; **new** `src/sentiment_superindex/analysis/cftc_event_gate.py`, `scripts/build_cftc_six_point_report.py`; frontend `server/utils/sentiment-mapper.ts`, `test/sentiment-mapper.spec.ts`.
   - Prod impact: **yes** — threshold + display change and two data-path fixes. Logged in `dev_to_prod_migration_todos.md`.

   - Summary: Implemented the plan from entry 1 across both repos. **Phase 0 — test harness:** the Nuxt repo had no runner at all; added `vitest` + `@vue/test-utils` + `@vitejs/plugin-vue` + `happy-dom` + `vue-tsc` with `npm run test` / `test:watch` / `typecheck`, plus a golden fixture captured from the live dev API (`test/fixtures/analyst-alerts.json`, 13 alerts covering all 8 shapes). This also surfaced a pre-existing `test/data-updated-at.spec.ts` that had never been executed — it passes. **Phase 1 — deleted the fabricated frontend:** `server/utils/overwatch-panel.ts` went from 194 lines to 31, dropping `degradationToPanelAlert`, `inferPattern`, `inferRecommendation`, `mockRunicPanelAlert`, `runicPanelAlertFromNightly`, `buildOverwatchPanelPayload` and `buildSystemChecks` — all of it dead (`parseForwardTestingRows` returned `[]` unconditionally) or fabricated (the interpolated `(win-backtest)/3` sparkline, and five system checks hardcoded to "warn · unavailable"). `parseForwardTestingRows` deleted from `signal-parsers.ts`; the BFF-side runic alert removed, which was the source of the **duplicate Combo F card** (`runic-dominant-f` vs backend `runic-f`). Badge string corrected to the spec's `'Overwatch · auto-triggered'`. **Phase 2 — render what the backend already sends:** `OverwatchAlertType` widened to all 8 backend types; tab filters now use the server's `channel` instead of a two-type whitelist, so `runic_watch` / `regime_warning` / `persistence` finally appear on MACRO; badges read from `meta.tabs`; **Analog Finder implemented** (spec's blue `#4A9EFF` block, up to 5 instances + median/worst/best/hit-rate summary) — the backend had always sent `historical_analogs` and the macro card never rendered `alert.html`, so it had been invisible since it shipped; list capped at 25 with a show-more control. **Phase 3 — backend data correctness:** new `position_risk` alert type splits the 237 portfolio triggers from the 5 genuine win-rate drifts (`analyst_service.py:101` was coalescing `profit_pct` into `fwd_rate`, so a position down 23.7% rendered as "FWD WR −23.7% · BELOW 60% FLOOR"); `above_floor` now honours `floor_pct` instead of a hardcoded `61.0`; `check_degradation` caches per floor so `?floor_pct=` stops being inert; `_first_sentence()` fixes the brief truncating mid-decimal (`"…with a 78."`); new `overwatch_schedule.py` populates `meta.next_signal_check` / `next_macro_scan`; signals badge now reads "5 drift watch · 237 position alerts" instead of a misleading "242 watch active". **Phase 4 — wired the endpoints that had zero consumers:** BFF switched to `/analytics/analyst/context` (one call for alerts + badges + regime + sentiment), SYSTEM tab now reads the real `/system/health` 7 checks, dashboard snippet reads `/analytics/analyst/brief`, chat sends `page_context` (route / tab / open alert ids / dominant combo — accepted by the backend since v1.8.1, never sent), and the **15 Jul spec D4 agent slide-in** is implemented against the live-but-unconsumed `GET /portfolio/alerts`: auto-open after 1.5s on the target page, badge dot only elsewhere, dismissal persisted in `sessionStorage` so a dismissed alert never re-opens in the same session. **Phase 5 — push mode (user chose option B):** Overwatch scans now run on the API's own event loop (`overwatch_runner.py`, no new dependency — APScheduler is not installed) instead of cron, because `overwatch_event_bus` is per-process and a cron interpreter published into an empty subscriber set; `OverwatchEventBus.bind_loop()` lets a scan thread deliver via `run_coroutine_threadsafe`. **Verified end to end: a scan running in a worker thread was received by a live SSE subscriber** — the first time push has ever delivered. Disable with `OVERWATCH_SCHEDULER=0`.
   - **New defect found and fixed during testing:** the smoke test caught **duplicate alert ids — 166 unique out of 249**. One combo commonly holds many simultaneous open positions (SFTBY alone had 29 live short breaches on the same function/interval), and the id keyed only on function/direction/interval/symbol. Added `entry_date`/`exit_date` to the portfolio alert payload and to the id. This broke Vue list keys and the panel's new-alert diff, and the pre-existing `deg-` ids had the same flaw.

`mockRunicPanelAlert()` still present (gated off); `pageContext`/`setContext` set from `constants/terminal-configs.ts:446` but never sent despite `context.chat.supports_page_context: true`; suggested prompts and the "Next check · Weekly · Mon 08:00 NZDT" footers are hardcoded while `meta.next_signal_check`/`next_macro_scan` return `null`. **Not running at all:** `scripts/overwatch/run_overwatch_{signals,macro,system}.py` have no crontab entry and no systemd timer, and even if scheduled they could not work — `overwatch_event_bus.py:59` is an in-process asyncio bus, so a separate cron process publishes into an empty subscriber set (spec open question 2, Redis, was never answered); `/analytics/analyst/fwd-trend` (P0 in `docs/AI_ANALYST_BACKEND_REQUIREMENTS.md` §5.2) returns 404; Claude alert copy is gated on `ANALYST_USE_CLAUDE_COPY`, absent from dev `.env`, so all alert text is template. **Spec violation:** the ALL badge reads `'Overwatch · Claude triggered'` (`useOverwatch.ts:44`) — the spec explicitly requires `'Overwatch · auto-triggered'`; the backend gets this right, the frontend overrides it. 2. **`POST /macro/run-nightly` could still clobber the live snapshot — guard + test DB isolation (API 1.10.9)** — SUCCESSFUL
   - Summary: Closes the half of the 2026-08-17 snapshot bug (entries 11/13) that `run_nightly(persist=…)` did **not** cover. `trigger_nightly_run()` passed any `as_of` straight into `run_nightly()`, which persists, so `POST /macro/run-nightly {"as_of": "2024-09-18"}` reproduced the exact corruption over HTTP — no pytest needed. Now persists only when `as_of` is unset or equals today; the response reports `persisted` and `persist_skipped_reason`, and `output_path` is `null` when skipped. Second fix in the same commit: the runic schema test no longer writes the shared macro DB — `setUp` copies the live DB to a tmpdir and points `MACRO_INTEL_DB` at it (copy, not empty, so the run does not re-download every series). Before this, each run left 2024-09-18 rows in `daily_readings` (12), `macro_regime_log` (1), `cftc_positioning` (1) and `emission_vectors` (12).
   - **Live proof on dev `:8507`:** `POST /macro/run-nightly {"as_of":"2024-09-18"}` → `persisted: false`, `output_path: null`, `persist_skipped_reason: "as_of=2024-09-18 is not today (2026-08-18); live snapshot left untouched"`; `runic_output.json` mtime **unchanged**, `date` still `2026-08-17`, no `runic_briefing_2024-09-18.*` emitted. DB row counts re-checked after the full suite: still 12/1/1/12 — no new pollution.
   - **Robust test + dev deploy (skill run, 2026-08-18):** branch `chatbot-dev`; `from api.main import app` OK. `pytest tests/test_api_macro.py -q` → **39 passed**; `pytest tests/test_runic_output_schema.py -q` → **2 passed** with the live DB mtime unchanged; `pytest tests/ -q` → **818 passed, 4 skipped, 0 failed** (249s). **The long-standing Monday-only failure is gone** — `test_shortlist_mtm_not_stale_zero_for_aged_signals` passes now that the 2026-08-17 pull landed, so the suite is fully green for the first time in this sequence. Mock audit on both changed API files clean. API surface changed → `API_VERSION` bumped **1.10.8 → 1.10.9**, endpoint page + changelog updated, OpenAPI re-exported. Dev API restarted (`smoke-test-apis.sh` 11/11 PASS, dev reports v1.10.9, prod v1.8.1 isolated and not restarted). Transient note: immediately after two back-to-back heavy `run-nightly` POSTs the follow-up GETs returned empty bodies; on retry all returned 200 — rate limiting, not a regression.
   - Files changed: `api/services/macro_service.py`, `api/routers/macro.py`, `api/main.py`, `tests/test_api_macro.py`, `tests/test_runic_output_schema.py`, `docs/mindwealth-api-docs/{changelog.md,services/macro/endpoints/post-run-nightly.md,openapi/mindwealth-v1.json}`
   - Git: submodule `divsum127/mindwealth-api-docs` `main` @ **`1121b3e`**; parent `chatbot-dev` @ **`40fda07de`** (includes the submodule pointer), author `divsum127`, both pushed. Only this change's hunks were staged — `macro_service.py` and `routers/macro.py` still carry ~238 uncommitted lines belonging to other tasks in this worktree.
   - Not fixed (deliberate): a backdated run still writes date-stamped rows to the **runic DB** through the API path (`pull_all_series` / `run_persistence_scan` run before the persist branch) — only the served snapshot is guarded; documented on the endpoint page. The 26 pre-existing 2024-09-18 rows were left in place rather than deleted, since they are real historical readings and a delete is riskier than the residue.
   - Prod impact: all 5 tracked files plus the submodule pointer merge `chatbot-dev` → `chatbot-prod`; response change is additive. No `.env`, schema, migration, or Nuxt work. Recorded in `dev_to_prod_migration_todos.md`.

3. **Landing page (`:8514`) showed "Could not fetch from server" on all 4 hero tiles — root cause + fix** — SUCCESSFUL
   - Root cause: commit `7661255` ("feat(bff): auth cookie gate and rate limits on Nitro API") in `/home/ubuntu/MindwealthUI_Vue` made `server/middleware/bff-auth.ts` require the `mw_access_token` cookie on every `/api/*` Nitro route except the `/api/v1` FastAPI proxy, but shipped `isPublicBffPath()` as a permanent `return false` — the public-path allowlist was never populated. The landing page `/` is public (`middleware/auth.global.ts` `PUBLIC_PATHS`), so its SSR fetches of `/api/performance` and `/api/runic/nightly` came back **401 Authentication required**, `resolveApiData` never ran, and `useLandingStats` fell through to `UNAVAILABLE_FETCH` for AVG WIN RATE / AVG 10YR CAGR / FUNCTIONS / MACRO COMBOS.
   - Fix: added a public, scalars-only BFF route `GET /api/landing-stats` (avg_win_rate, avg_cagr, avg_sharpe, function_count, macro_combo_count) and allowlisted just that path in `isPublicBffPath()`. `/api/performance` and `/api/runic/nightly` stay gated — they carry full signal rows and macro combos and must not be public. Route caches in-process (5 min live / 30 s when upstream is down) so anonymous landing traffic cannot hammer `loadPerformance()`, which fans out to 4 upstream calls. `useLandingStats` now reads the new route.
   - Verified on dev `:8514` after rebuild + `systemctl restart mindwealth-ui-dev`: `GET /api/landing-stats` → 200 `{"data_source":"live","avg_win_rate":75.92,"avg_cagr":11.9,"avg_sharpe":0,"function_count":9,"macro_combo_count":7}`; `/api/performance` and `/api/runic/nightly` still 401 without a cookie; landing HTML renders `75.92%`, `11.9%`, `9`, `7` with zero occurrences of "Could not fetch from server". `nuxi typecheck` errors went 49 → 47 (both removed errors were mine; the other 47 are pre-existing in unrelated files).
   - Files changed (repo `/home/ubuntu/MindwealthUI_Vue`, branch `ui-dev`, **uncommitted**): `server/api/landing-stats.get.ts` (new), `server/utils/require-auth.ts`, `composables/useLandingStats.ts`, `types/api.ts` (added `LandingStatsResponse`)
   - Prod impact: **yes, prod `:8512` (www.mindwealth.co) has the identical bug** — its landing page still returns 1 match for "Could not fetch from server". Both services run from the same `WorkingDirectory=/home/ubuntu/MindwealthUI_Vue` and the same `.output`, so the fix is already built on disk; prod is serving the pre-fix bundle only because its node process started 2026-08-17. Restarting `mindwealth-ui.service` will pick it up. Not restarted — waiting on user go-ahead. See `dev_to_prod_migration_todos.md`.

4. **Chatbot audit gaps 5, 6, 8 — vocabulary and macro data** — SUCCESSFUL
   - Summary: Fixed the three routing/data gaps from entry 18's audit, all the same shape as the original NZ complaint — the assistant could not reach or name data we already compute. **(5)** "give me a short summary about claude report" routed CONVERSATIONAL and the model asked whether Claude was a **stock ticker**, while `claude_report` is a first-class signal type backed by a 112 KB file refreshed daily. **(6)** "What signal types exist?" returned a textbook LONG/SHORT answer instead of our taxonomy or any function name. **(8)** "what is the current macro regime and which combo is dominant?" routed `WEB_RAG` and answered "transitional with mixed signals" from news sources while Runic had **Combo F dominant, week 20 of 26, TACTICAL EASY MONEY** — the word *combo* is MindWealth-only vocabulary and still did not force internal. Fixes: two new deterministic router overrides beside the existing level/recommendation pair (`_PLATFORM_VOCAB_RE` for our own nouns, `_MACRO_QUERY_RE` for regime/combos/SSI/breadth/VIX/CAPE/sizing), both of which only ever turn internal ON so web stays where the router put it; **platform-vocab questions are additionally demoted out of CONVERSATIONAL**, which was the actual reason gap 6 survived the first attempt — that route skips every override and answers from general knowledge. New **SOURCE D** (`chatbot/macro_context.py`) feeds the Runic nightly regime, active/watch combos, SSI summary and portfolio risk over HTTP, labelling WATCH combos as *not firing* so they cannot be read as active signals, and instructing the model to surface disagreement with the web rather than adopt the web's regime. New **SOURCE E** (`chatbot/platform_context.py`) is local and cheap: our five signal types with descriptions and file freshness, plus the live function list read once from the entry CSV, injected on the hybrid, internal **and** conversational paths.
   - Verification: `pytest tests/ chatbot/tests -q` → **900 passed, 4 skipped**; 17 new cases covering override composition, the conversational demotion, HTTP-failure degradation and the WATCH-combo labelling. Live on dev after each fix — claude report now returns a real summary of the 2026-08-17 report (VIX 15.19, 72 signals); signal types returns INTERNAL with `Platform Capabilities` in the flow, all five types selected and our real function names; macro regime returns **HYBRID** with a `Macro Overlay` step and leads with Combo F week 20 of 26. Dev API restarted, `smoke-test-apis.sh` 11/11 PASS, prod isolation intact.
   - Files changed: `chatbot/agents/llm_router.py`, `chatbot/chatbot_engine.py`, `chatbot/config.py`, `chatbot/macro_context.py` (new), `chatbot/platform_context.py` (new), `chatbot/tests/test_platform_and_macro_context.py` (new)
   - Found while testing, **not** a code defect: `mindwealth-api-dev.service` runs uvicorn with `--reload`, so **every file save kills in-flight chat answers**. Three live replays died mid-flight while another session edited `api/`. Prod does not use `--reload` (verified), so this is dev-only — but it makes live chat testing unreliable whenever anyone is editing the repo. The new `fail_orphaned()` turned it into an honest 8-second error instead of a hang, which is how it was noticed.
   - Also noted: `MINDWEALTH_API_BASE_URL` is empty by default, so SOURCE C/D derive their base URL from `API_PORT`. systemd sets it per service (dev 8507, prod 8506), but **any invocation outside the service inherits no `API_PORT` and silently queries prod** — the fallback is 8506. Bit me once during testing; recorded in the migration todos.
   - Prod impact: **yes** — six files merge `chatbot-dev` → `chatbot-prod` plus an API restart. No new env keys (all three flags default on), no API surface change.
   - Still open from the audit: gaps 1-4 (MTM anchored on `Signal Open Price` vs the entry price shown, ~90% duplicate rows in `entry.csv`, MTM recomputed instead of quoted, no sample-size caveat on ranked screens) and gap 7 (stale web quotes shown beside live ones).

5. **Removed `--reload` from the dev API unit + full 12-query live regression** — SUCCESSFUL
   - Summary: `mindwealth-api-dev.service` ran `uvicorn ... --reload`, which watches the whole repo. Chat jobs run in worker threads **inside** that process, so a reload is indistinguishable from a crash: the job dies mid-answer and the user is told the analyst is unreachable. Three live replays died this way in a single session while another process was editing `api/`. Removed from both `/etc/systemd/system/mindwealth-api-dev.service` (host) and the tracked copy `scripts/mindwealth-api-dev.service`, then `daemon-reload` + restart. Prod never had `--reload`, so this only aligns dev with prod. The convenience was worth little here: the box is shared, and the API is restarted explicitly after every deploy anyway.
   - Verification — **the failure mode itself**: started a deep-dive answer, then touched `api/main.py`, `chatbot/config.py` and `api/services/analyst_service.py` while it was running. **Zero server restarts**, and the answer completed at **125.6s with 16,271 chars**. Under `--reload` that answer would have been discarded.
   - Verification — **12-query live regression through the Nuxt BFF on `:8514`** with a real session cookie, **12/12 passed, zero errors**: ticker lookup 27.6s, SOXX MTM 20.1s, top-5 Sharpe screen 35.1s, "should i buy meta" HYBRID+Conviction 47.7s, the NZ replacement question HYBRID+Conviction 50.2s, macro regime **HYBRID+Macro Overlay** 40.1s, signal types **INTERNAL+Platform Capabilities** 52.9s, claude report **INTERNAL+Platform Capabilities** 52.8s, BRK-B signals+news HYBRID 37.6s, exits INTERNAL+Conviction 90.4s (3 browser resumes), plus a two-turn continuity check where "now calculate the mtm according to the last trade" resolved from session context, and `GET /api/chat/history` → **200 with 4 messages**. Every long answer used the resume path; none 503'd.
   - `pytest tests/ chatbot/tests -q` → **900 passed, 4 skipped**; `smoke-test-apis.sh` **11/11 PASS**, prod isolation intact.
   - Files changed: `scripts/mindwealth-api-dev.service`; host unit `/etc/systemd/system/mindwealth-api-dev.service` edited directly (not in git).
   - Prod impact: **none to prod runtime** — prod already runs without `--reload`. The tracked unit file merges with the rest of the branch for consistency; the host-side edit is dev-only and needs no prod action.

6. **Chatbot bug sweep against the `mindwealth todos` sheet + audit gaps — 9 cases replayed, 1 new fix, 3 new defects found** — SUCCESSFUL
   - Source: read the `v2_TODOs` tab (read-only) and pulled every chatbot-related row — C3 (Google/NVDA levels answered from the web), C11 (Claude shortlist stuck on 26 June), C12 (MTM showing 0.0 for MHCI/TLK/BRBK), C13/C14 (confirmation status and signal-date questions), C16 (raw Anthropic billing error rendered to the user) — then replayed them with the still-open audit gaps 1, 3, 4 and 7.
   - **FIXED and verified:** **C3** — "recent exit levels and entry levels for google and nvda" now routes **INTERNAL with `web_search_used: false`**; no death cross, no web resistance levels, which was Rohit's explicit complaint ("when we are driving a ferrari we dont want a fiat"). **C11** — the Claude shortlist question was still broken at the start of this sweep (see below), now returns the real report date **2026-08-17** with its age. **C12** — MTM is populated, e.g. MCHI TRENDPULSE **−2.62%** over 7 days, not 0.0. **C13/C14** — quarterly signal confirmation status and dates answer from internal data (300 rows, entry+exit+targets).
   - **NEW FIX this sweep:** `_PLATFORM_VOCAB_RE` required `claude` and `report` to be adjacent, so Rohit's actual wording — "claude **shortlisted** report" — fell through to CONVERSATIONAL and got "I don't see any Claude shortlisted report in the current context". Pattern widened to `claude` + optional qualifier + `report|analysis|shortlist`, plus bare "shortlisted signals", in both the router override and the SOURCE E gate. Verified live: now INTERNAL + `Platform Capabilities`.
   - **NEW DEFECT 1 — non-deterministic ticker omission (highest severity found).** The Google/NVDA query fetched **both** tickers (`assets: [GOOG, NVDA]`, 17 rows, entry+exit) yet the first run's answer stated "**there are no recent entry or exit signals for Google (GOOG)**" and covered NVDA only. GOOG actually has **15 open entry signals**, latest 2026-07-16 PULSEGAUGE at −3.54%. A second identical run included GOOG correctly. Retrieval is right; the model silently drops a requested ticker some of the time. Same shape as the NZ answer flip-flop noted in entry 18 — this is now two confirmed instances, and it is the failure mode most likely to be read as the system lying.
   - **NEW DEFECT 2 — silent ticker substitution.** "mhci, tlk and brbk" resolved to `[MCHI, TLT, BRK-B]`. `TLK` is not in the universe; `TLT` is a completely different asset (20y Treasury ETF vs Telkom Indonesia). The answer happened to be correct — the model kept TLT's rows separate and reported "TLK … may not be currently tracked" — but the extractor put an unrequested asset into `assets`, and a less careful synthesis would have published TLT's numbers under TLK's name.
   - **NEW DEFECT 3 — raw provider errors still reach users.** `chatbot_engine.py:940,3149,3294,3554` all format `f"Error processing query: {str(e)}"`, which is how C16's Anthropic billing message and its `request_id` were rendered into the chat. Unchanged; needs a sanitiser that logs the detail and shows a neutral message.
   - **Still open, re-confirmed with evidence:** **gap 1** — when *asked* "show me the entry price you used" the bot is fully transparent ("Signal Open Price $492.42 … differs from the signal price of $507.68"), but unprompted it writes "13.55% above entry" while displaying entry as $507.68. It is a labelling default, not a calculation error. **gap 3** — MTM recomputed (−0.11%) rather than quoted (−0.14%). **gap 4** — sample size *is* reported when explicitly requested (14 trades, 5 trades) but never volunteered, so `Sharpe 7.88 / 100% win rate` still reads as unqualified. **gap 7** — the model now warns that web quotes "may reflect different timestamps" but does not say the NVDA quote is a month old. Gaps 1, 3, 4 and 7 are all prompt-level fixes; gap 2 (~90% duplicate rows) is upstream data.
   - Verification: `pytest tests/ chatbot/tests -q` → **900 passed, 4 skipped**. All nine replays ran through the Nuxt BFF on `:8514` with a real session cookie, zero errors, zero 503s, 10–53s each.
   - Files changed: `chatbot/agents/llm_router.py`, `chatbot/platform_context.py`
   - Prod impact: **yes** — both files merge with the rest of the chatbot work; no new env keys, no API surface change.

7. **Remaining chatbot defects — five fixes with edge-case guards, plus root cause of the MTM disagreement** — SUCCESSFUL
   - **(1) Raw provider errors reaching users** (sheet C16). New `chatbot/error_messages.py` maps exceptions to fixed safe strings and logs the original. Every branch returns a **constant**, so provider text cannot leak through a partial regex match — asserted by a test that plants a token in the exception and checks both a matching and a non-matching branch. Applied at all six engine sites and in `api/jobs/runner.py`, where `error` is exactly what the Nuxt client renders; the raw exception survives as `error_detail` on the job record.
   - **(2) Silent ticker substitution.** "mhci, tlk and brbk" → `[MCHI, TLT, BRK-B]`. Deliberately **nothing is dropped**: inference is usually right ("google" → GOOG, "brbk" → BRK-B) and dropping would break those cases. Instead the prompt names the inferred symbols and forbids presenting one symbol's rows under another's name. Live result: TLK is now reported as untracked in its own section while MCHI and BRK-B are answered normally.
   - **(3) Silently dropped ticker.** `_apply_coverage_guard` compares the answer against the requested symbols and appends a factual note when one that **had rows** is never mentioned. Skips inferred symbols (so correctly ignoring a wrong guess is not flagged), zero-row symbols, and region filters like `.NZ`; matches on non-alphanumeric boundaries so `BRK-B`, `^TNX` and `000660.KS` work and `AAPLE` does not count as `AAPL`; wrapped so it can never raise into an answer. **Live: GOOG appeared in 3/3 runs of the query that previously dropped it, with the guard firing zero false positives.**
   - **(4) Duplicate rows.** `entry.csv` holds 25,709 rows for 2,671 unique symbol+function pairs. Byte-identical rows are now dropped before the 100-row cap, at both cap sites. Only rows equal in **every** column are removed, so two genuine signals sharing a symbol both survive; unhashable cells degrade to the original frame.
   - **(5) Numbers without context.** Five system-prompt rules: name the price MTM is measured from, copy the stored MTM rather than recompute, quote sample size with every Sharpe/win rate plus a caution under 20 trades or at exactly 100%, date every web quote, and give every requested symbol its own section. **Live: unprompted, the top-5 screen now says "Sharpe 7.88 (14 trades)" followed by an explicit small-sample warning.**
   - **ROOT CAUSE FOUND for the MTM disagreement (was audit gap 3, previously attributed to the model).** The chatbot is **not** recomputing. Asked to quote the field character-for-character it returns `-0.11%, 3 days` — because that is what it receives. `trade_store/US/2026-08-17_outstanding_signal.csv` and `_all_signal.csv` carry **−0.11%** for AAPL FRACTAL TRACK 2026-08-14, while `chatbot/data/entry.csv` carries **−0.14%** for the same signal with the **same** `Signal Open Price` of 306.028. −0.14% is measured from the open (306.028), −0.11% from the signal price (305.93). **Two platform exports disagree with each other**; the chatbot loads open positions from `outstanding_signal.csv` first, exactly as its prompt documents, and reports faithfully. This is a data-contract question for Rohit, in the same family as gap 1, and was deliberately **not** changed unilaterally.
   - Verification: **930 passed, 4 skipped** (30 new edge-case tests). `smoke-test-apis.sh` 11/11, prod isolation intact. Live regression through `:8514` after deploy — exits (72.8s), macro regime HYBRID + Macro Overlay (35.1s), buy recommendation HYBRID + Conviction (47.7s), claude report INTERNAL + Platform Capabilities (47.7s), all clean with no coverage-note false positives.
   - **Self-inflicted incident worth recording:** the first version of prompt rule 2 contained an apostrophe ("the platform's own number") inside the single-quoted `SYSTEM_PROMPT` literal, which broke `prompts/engine.py` and took the dev API down for ~2 minutes on restart. Caught by the next live call returning `err: true` in 0.0s. Fixed by rewording. Lesson: `prompts/engine.py` stores prompts as single-quoted Python literals, so any inserted copy must avoid raw apostrophes; a syntax check (`ast.parse`) before restart is now part of my flow.
   - Files changed: `chatbot/error_messages.py` (new), `chatbot/asset_coverage.py` (new), `chatbot/tests/test_error_and_coverage_guards.py` (new), `chatbot/chatbot_engine.py`, `chatbot/smart_data_fetcher.py`, `prompts/engine.py`, `api/jobs/runner.py`
   - Prod impact: **yes** — all seven files merge `chatbot-dev` → `chatbot-prod` plus an API restart. No new env keys, no API surface change.

8. **MTM basis, ticker/alias map, and web-quote staleness — the last three open defects** — SUCCESSFUL
   - **(A) MTM now measures from the signal price** (product decision). Two exports disagreed on one position: `*_outstanding_signal.csv` **−0.11%** vs `chatbot/data/entry.csv` **−0.14%** for AAPL FRACTAL TRACK 2026-08-14. **Core was always right** — it computes `(close_now − close_at_signal)/close_at_signal` and never uses `Signal Open Price` for MTM; **this repo** flipped the basis in `resolve_signal_basis` and the nightly rebase wrote the re-based number back over core's. So the fix removes an override rather than redefining anything, and it matches `REPORTS.md`, which already documented `Signal Open Price` as a dedup key and explicitly not a display price. The fallback to the open price is kept on purpose: virtual-trading synthetic rows have no compound price and would otherwise collapse to `0.0%` — the sheet's "MTM shows 0.0" symptom. `test_prefers_signal_open_price_when_valid` asserted the old behaviour and was replaced. **Verified live: `GET /signals/surface` returns `mtm_pct: -0.11`.**
   - **(B) Committed ticker/alias map replaces LLM guessing.** New `config/ticker_aliases.json` covers all **197** universe symbols (**178 named**), generated by `scripts/update_ticker_aliases.py` from the SEC ticker file + yfinance `longName` + the hand-curated NZ aliases Deep Research already used. **Ambiguous aliases are pruned** (16 dropped, e.g. `ishares`, which matched every iShares ETF — an ambiguous alias is a guess wearing a lookup table's clothes). Resolution order: exact → company name/alias → **adjacent-transposition typo** → unique base match. The transposition step is deliberately narrow: a neighbour swap is a finger slip (`mhci`→MCHI, `brbk`→BRK-B) while a substitution turns a ticker into a different company, so `tlk`→`tlt` stays unresolved. **Membership alone cannot catch that** — `TLT` is a real universe symbol — so `verify_extracted_symbols` adds provenance: a symbol survives only if the question (or conversation history, for follow-ups) contains it literally or a token resolving to exactly it. `unresolved_tickers`, written by the extractor and **read by nobody since it was added**, now reaches the prompt.
   - **(C) Every web source is dated; stale ones are barred from prices.** `SearchResult` carried no date field at all, so the only temporal signal was `Retrieved at: <now>` — the fetch time. Tavily populates `published_date` for `topic="news"` and the SDK passes it through untouched, so it was already arriving and being discarded. Now requested (gated on the existing recency heuristic), with fallbacks to a date in the URL (`/2026/07/18/`, `/20260718/`) then the snippet, reusing `tools/event_date_extractor`. Age is measured against **our data as-of**, not wall clock, since the question is whether a quote is older than the prices beside it. Sources older than `WEB_QUOTE_MAX_AGE_DAYS` (default 2) — **or undated**, because guessing freshness caused this — are tagged unusable for prices but kept for narrative.
   - **Three defects the tests/live runs caught in my own work:** an ISO regex that only counted digits accepted `2026-13-45` (range validation added); the provenance check initially dropped `MCHI` because a recovered typo is not literal text (resolver matches now count as provenance); and the mapping notice named inferred symbols without saying **which user word produced each**, so the model reported `BRBK` as a separate untracked ticker beside `BRK-B`'s own data (pairing added).
   - Verification: `pytest tests/ chatbot/tests -q` → **971 passed, 4 skipped**, plus 2 failures in `tests/test_ssi_vix_ratio.py` that **reproduce with my changes stashed** (another session's in-flight SSI work). 39 new tests across the three parts. Live battery **12/12** through `:8514` (one run aborted mid-flight by a concurrent Nuxt restart and passed on retry). `smoke-test-apis.sh` 11/11, prod isolation intact.
   - Files changed: `src/utils/mtm_pricing.py`, `tests/test_mtm_pricing.py`, `config/ticker_aliases.json` (new), `scripts/update_ticker_aliases.py` (new), `chatbot/{ticker_resolver,unified_extractor,asset_coverage,chatbot_engine,config}.py`, `chatbot/agents/web_search_agent.py`, `prompts/engine.py`, `chatbot/tests/test_{ticker_alias_map,web_quote_staleness,error_and_coverage_guards}.py`
   - Prod impact: **yes** — commits `0772d7dab`, `fd4d0f676`, `e3156ee35`. **Numbers on the live site change** with (A): any position whose signal price differs from its open price now shows a different MTM. Optional env key `WEB_QUOTE_MAX_AGE_DAYS`. `entry.csv` rows keep their stored MTM until the nightly rebase re-runs.
   - Still open, unchanged: `portfolio_pipeline_service.py:610` prefers the sizer's `pnl_pct` (virtual-trading basis) over the MTM column — a fourth basis for the same position, and its own decision.

9. **Full chatbot verification sweep — every historically broken query replayed** — SUCCESSFUL
   - Replayed **18 distinct cases** through the Nuxt BFF on `:8514` with a real session cookie: all 4 sheet bugs (C3 web contamination, C12 MTM/TLK, C11 shortlist date, C13 confirmation), Rohit's original NZ question, the 503 chat, all 8 audit gaps, the deep dive, exits, ticker lookup, a recommendation, both adversarial security prompts, and a two-turn continuity check. **18/18 pass.** Four showed a transient failure caused by a concurrent API restart mid-flight and passed on retry.
   - **MTM verified in bulk, not by sample:** compared **every** row of `GET /signals/surface` against core's own `2026-08-17_outstanding_signal.csv` on the exact compound key (symbol + function + interval). **71/71 exact match.** An earlier 61/71 result was an artefact of my own comparison key colliding on tickers with multiple signals — the per-row comparison is clean.
   - **Determinism:** the query that used to silently drop a ticker (`google and nvda`) ran **3/3 with both symbols present**; the coverage guard fired **zero false positives** across the entire sweep (`assets_not_covered` null everywhere).

7. **AI Analyst chat — buy/replace questions ignored Layer 1 signals & conviction; "Could not reach the analyst" + vanished history** — SUCCESSFUL
   - Summary: Five distinct root causes behind two reported chats on dev (`:8514` → API `:8507`). **(R1)** "what new zealand stocks do i buy to replace fph and mft" routed to **WEB_RAG**, which answers purely from Tavily text and `return`s at `chatbot_engine.py:2571-2587` — SmartDataFetcher, SynthesisAgent and the calculator were all skipped. Evidence: `chatbot/jobs/35f6152b-e30a-42fc-9ae3-aee9ce98e9bc.json` (`route=WEB_RAG`, `intent_classified_by=llm_router`, queries dated **2023**). `ROUTER_SYSTEM` had rules for entry/exit/target/stop wording but none for recommendation/replacement, and no current date was passed to the router. **(R2)** The chatbot had **zero** access to conviction/fundamentals — structural, not a routing miss (`grep -i conviction chatbot/*.py` = 0 hits), despite `conviction_store/` being fresh (196 tickers) and fully served over HTTP. **(R3)** Bare `FPH`/`MFT` could never match stored `FPH.NZ`/`MFT.NZ` (exact `isin`, `.upper()` only), and the extractor prompt truncated the ticker list at 100 entries while `MFT.NZ` sits at index **111** — invisible to the model; `prefer_open_only` additionally excluded the very names the user said they had sold. **(R4)** The second chat's job `4326975d-…` **completed successfully in 147 s** but the client poll budget was `deep_research_total_timeout_seconds + 30` = 150 s and the Nuxt layer caches GETs for 30 s, so polls landed 30 s apart (01:50:54 → 01:52:54) and the 01:53:21 completion was never observed before the 01:53:24 deadline → 503 → red banner. **(R5)** History vanished because `save_history()` truncated in place then `json.dump`ed without `default=`; a pandas `Timestamp` raised mid-dump (`Error saving history: Object of type Timestamp is not JSON serializable`), leaving a corrupt file, and `load_history()` then silently reset the conversation to `[]` (`Error loading history: Expecting value: line 2271 column 23`). Fixes: router rules 9-10 + a deterministic `apply_recommendation_internal_override` that forces internal **while leaving web on** so the route becomes HYBRID (signals = SOURCE A primary, web = SOURCE B supplementary); a new SOURCE C block feeding ranked buy/exit lists, real `Signal Quality Composite Score`s, conviction scores/verdicts and fundamentals over HTTP from our own API; a shared ticker resolver plus a base-symbol fallback in the fetcher; atomic `default=str` history writes with corrupt-file quarantine and per-session write locks; and `DEEP_RESEARCH_TOTAL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` 120 → 300 so the client budget becomes ~330 s. Verified: **44 chatbot tests pass** (14 new regression tests), SOURCE C builds all 5 sections against the live dev API, bare `['FPH','MFT']` now returns **665 rows where it returned 0**, and the `Timestamp` payload that used to corrupt history now round-trips. Notably the web-only answer's top pick **AIA.NZ carries `verdict=CANCEL BUY, conviction_score=-7`** in our own engine — the contradiction this fix removes.
   - Files changed: `prompts/engine.py`, `chatbot/agents/llm_router.py`, `chatbot/conviction_context.py` (new), `chatbot/tools/mindwealth_api_client.py` (new), `chatbot/ticker_resolver.py` (new), `chatbot/unified_extractor.py`, `chatbot/smart_data_fetcher.py`, `chatbot/history_manager.py`, `chatbot/chatbot_engine.py`, `chatbot/config.py`, `api/jobs/runner.py`, `chatbot/prompt_changelog.json` (LLM_ROUTER_SYSTEM v3), `chatbot/tests/test_recommendation_routing.py` (new), `chatbot/tests/test_history_durability.py` (new)
   - Prod impact: **yes** — merge `chatbot-dev` → `chatbot-prod`; new optional env keys; **one required frontend follow-up in `MindwealthUI_Vue` (out of this task's scope) without which the 503 can still recur.** See `docs/dev_to_prod_migration_todos.md`.
   - Not run: the live end-to-end LLM replay of both questions (user declined the paid call). Router/conviction/ticker/history layers were each verified independently instead; the final Claude-rendered answer is unverified.

9. **INCIDENT — `www.mindwealth.co` down 14m50s (self-inflicted `pkill`), plus deployed-frontend status audit** — SUCCESSFUL (service restored; incident caused by this session)
   - **Incident.** While cleaning up the `:3007` smoke-test server from task #8, `pkill -f ".output/server/index.mjs"` was run. That pattern **also matched both systemd-managed Nuxt services**, because `mindwealth-ui.service` (prod, `:8512`) and `mindwealth-ui-dev.service` (dev, `:8514`) both run `node .output/server/index.mjs` from the **same** `WorkingDirectory=/home/ubuntu/MindwealthUI_Vue`. Both received SIGTERM and exited cleanly at **12:14:06 UTC** (`Deactivated successfully`, `ExecMainStatus=0`). Both units are `Restart=on-failure`, and a clean SIGTERM exit is **not** a failure — so **systemd did not auto-restart either** (`NRestarts=0`). `nginx` maps `mindwealth.co www.mindwealth.co` → `127.0.0.1:8512`, so **the public site was down**. Dev (`http://51.20.53.218` → `:8514`) was restarted by another operator at 12:18:57 (4m51s down). Prod stayed down until `sudo systemctl start mindwealth-ui.service` in this session at **12:28:56** — **total prod outage 14m50s (12:14:06 → 12:28:56)**. Verified restored: `:8512` active and listening, `https://www.mindwealth.co/` → 200.
   - **Root cause of the blast radius:** the `pkill` pattern was written for a one-off local test server without checking whether any systemd unit ran the same command line. `ps aux`/`systemctl list-units` were only run *after* the fact. **Rule for next time: never `pkill -f` on a generic Node/Nitro entrypoint on this host — stop test servers by PID, or bind them and kill by port.** Both Nuxt services share one entrypoint string, so any `-f .output/server/index.mjs` match is a two-service outage.
   - **Second self-inflicted change:** `npm run build` in task #8 wrote to `/home/ubuntu/MindwealthUI_Vue/.output` — the **live artifact directory both deployed services run from** (mtime `12:11:14`). The previous deployed build was overwritten and is unrecoverable. It happens to be benign (branch was already `ui-dev`, and the built bundle does contain `0921dd3`'s new symbols — `mapComboPriorityOrder`, `SIGMA_SOURCE_LABELS`, `demoted_for_low_n`, `model_barrier_basis`, `min_matured_episodes` all present in `.output`), but the build read sources **mid-edit** (the 6 files were being written 12:09:48–12:10:31; build finished 12:11:14), so the artifact is a mid-edit snapshot, **not provably identical to any commit**. A clean rebuild from `0921dd3` + restart is recommended; not performed (needs consent — it restarts prod).
   - **Deployed frontend status audit — everything currently UP and functional:**
     | Service | Port | Public entry | Upstream API | Status |
     |---|---|---|---|---|
     | `mindwealth-ui.service` (prod) | 8512 | `www.mindwealth.co` | `127.0.0.1:8506` → v**1.8.1**, `conviction_store` = prod clone | active ✅ |
     | `mindwealth-ui-dev.service` (dev) | 8514 | `http://51.20.53.218` | `127.0.0.1:8507` → v**1.10.8**, `conviction_store` = git clone | active ✅ |
     All 10 page routes correct on **both** (`/` + `/login` 200; the 8 gated pages 302 → login). BFF gate correct on both (`/api/dashboard` 401). `/api/v1` proxy live on both (`/health` 200, `/auth/me` 401). SSR renders real content (`<title>MindWealth · Alpha Terminal</title>`, 115,614 bytes) and the client bundle serves (`/_nuxt/DQtDe5-N.js` 200, 230 KB). `http://www.mindwealth.co` → 301 → `https://` → 200.
   - **Structural finding (pre-existing, not caused here): prod and dev Nuxt share one working tree and one `.output`.** Both units point at `/home/ubuntu/MindwealthUI_Vue`. SSR byte sizes on `:8512` and `:8514` are **identical (115,614)** — proof they serve the same artifact. Consequences: (a) "prod" `www.mindwealth.co` serves whatever the **`ui-dev`** branch last built — there is no separate prod build, and `presentation-prod` (at `ba2bcfd`) is not what the public site runs; (b) any `npm run build` in that directory silently redeploys the public site's assets on the next restart; (c) a rebuild without an immediate restart breaks live asset loading, because Nitro serves hashed files from `.output/public` and the running process references hashes that no longer exist. The only real isolation between prod and dev is the systemd `NUXT_API_BASE_URL` (8506 vs 8507).
   - Files changed: `docs/mindwealth_ui_job_status.md`, `docs/mindwealth_ui_repo_job_status_details.md`, `docs/dev_to_prod_migration_todos.md` (logging only).
   - Prod impact: **outage occurred and was resolved within this task**; no `MindWealth_UI` code touched. Two follow-ups recorded in `dev_to_prod_migration_todos.md`: the shared prod/dev build directory, and the clean rebuild of the mid-edit artifact.

11. **Dev site `:8514` showing a wrong/stale "as of" datetime (~13–14 Aug while the calendar reads 17–18 Aug)** — SUCCESSFUL (investigation only, no code changes yet)
   - Summary: Two independent date sources feed the dev UI; one is a genuine defect. **(A) Real bug — the test suite overwrites the live macro snapshot.** `tests/test_runic_output_schema.py:34` calls `run_nightly(as_of="2024-09-18", use_claude=False)`, and `run_nightly` unconditionally persists at `src/macro_intelligence/jobs/nightly_run.py:262` → `write_runic_json(payload)` → `src/macro_intelligence/output/json_writer.py:150` → `json_output_path()` (`src/macro_intelligence/config.py:31`) → the **live, gitignored** `macro_intelligence/output/runic_output.json`. The test has no tmpdir, no monkeypatch and does not set the already-supported `MACRO_INTEL_JSON_PATH` env override; `write_briefing()` is clobbered the same way. Timeline confirmed from mtimes (server clock is `Etc/UTC`): **18:02:42** cron `run_macro_nightly.py` wrote the correct snapshot (`runic_briefing_2026-08-17.html/pdf`); **18:19:47** the `pytest tests/` run logged in entry 10 above rewrote `runic_output.json` and emitted `runic_briefing_2024-09-18.html/pdf`; **18:21:13** the dev API restarted and loaded the clobbered file. Payload proof: the live JSON says Combo F **week 1** while `macro_intelligence/logs/nightly.log` from the real 18:02 run says **week 20**. Blast radius — every runic-JSON-backed endpoint now returns `date: 2024-09-18`: `/macro/status`, `/macro/regime`, `/macro/runic/nightly`, `/macro/runic/variables/current`, `/macro/overview/kpis`, `/macro/combos`, `/macro/combo/active`, `/macro/combo-c/cancel`, `/macro/combo-f/window`, `/macro/narrative`, `/macro/persistence`, `/macro/variables/heatmap`, `/macro/data/freshness`, `/macro/events/pre-catalyst`, `/macro/events/post-regime`, `/portfolio/sizer`, `/portfolio/sizing`. Unaffected (verified `2026-08-17`): `/macro/ssi/summary|history|multiplier`, `/macro/sentiment/positioning`, `/portfolio/risk` — those read `positioning.json`, a different writer. **(B) Not a bug — the topbar "last updated" reading Aug 14.** `GET /api/v1/meta` returns `2026-08-14T16:00:00-04:00`; `api/services/meta_service.py:29 resolve_report_date()` takes the latest dated `trade_store/US/*_outstanding_signal.csv`, and only `2026-08-14_outstanding_signal.csv` exists. The pull is `emailscript.sh` on `0 22 * * *` (22:00 UTC = 18:00 ET); it last ran Sun 2026-08-16 22:31 and correctly emitted the Fri 14 Aug trading day (15/16 = weekend), and Monday's run had not fired at the time of investigation (19:2x UTC). Expected behaviour, but the UI carries no "as of last trading day" label, so it reads 1–3 days behind every weekend and all of Monday daytime.
