15. **Chatbot "not responding" / "Tavily and other services offline" — chat history endpoint 500, plus a permanently-wrong India health check** — SUCCESSFUL
   - Summary: Reported as the chatbot erroring and external services looking down. **Neither was true of the services.** Live checks: **Tavily `ok`** (last search 1673ms), **Claude API `ok`** (267ms), Google Sheets `ok`, Macro agent `ok`, SSI `ok`. Two real defects behind the symptoms. **(1) `GET /chatbot/sessions/{id}/history?display=true` returned 500** — `PydanticSerializationError: Unable to serialize unknown type: <class 'pandas.core.frame.DataFrame'>`, 8 occurrences in 3h against session `6166cd19-…`, the only 500 on the box. `HistoryManager.load_history()` rebuilds `full_signal_tables` into pandas DataFrames (`history_manager.py:398-409`) because the engine needs them that way for later turns, and `chatbot_service.get_history()` handed them straight to the response serializer. The chat panel loads history on open **and on refresh**, so every open failed and the chat read as dead. **This was latent, not new** — before entry 7 made history writes durable, the files were corrupt and `load_history` reset to `[]`, so there were no tables to serialize and the endpoint returned 200 by accident; fixing persistence exposed it. Fixed with a deep sanitizer in `get_history` (both `display` modes): DataFrame → records, numpy scalars → python, `Timestamp`/`NaT` → isoformat or null, non-finite floats → null, unknown types → `str`. The engine's own in-memory copy keeps its DataFrames. **(2) India CSV pipeline health check was structurally broken** — it looked for `trade_store/India/data_fetch_datetime.json` while the directory on disk is **`INDIA`**, so on a case-sensitive filesystem it reported `path not found` permanently, a red SYSTEM row that said nothing about the pipeline. Both spellings are tried now. It still fails, but honestly: the India stamp file is dated **2026-02-11**, i.e. **~4503h (6 months) stale** — the India pipeline has not run since February, which is a real finding this check was hiding.
   - Verification: new regression test `TestHistorySerialization::test_get_history_sanitizes_dataframes` (frames + `np.int64`/`np.float64` + `NaT` + `NaN`, asserts JSON-encodability, null-not-`"nan"`, and that the engine's copy still holds a DataFrame). `pytest tests/test_api_chatbot.py -q` → **9 passed**; `pytest tests/ -q` → **811 passed, 1 failed, 3 skipped** (same pre-existing Monday-only `test_shortlist_mtm_not_stale_zero_for_aged_signals`). Dev API restarted; `smoke-test-apis.sh` **all PASS**, prod isolation intact. Live over HTTP on `:8507` with a minted admin token: history **200 in both display modes** (367 KB with `display=true`), `GET /system/health` returns Tavily `ok` / Claude `ok` / India `4503.3h ago`.
   - Files changed: `api/services/chatbot_service.py`, `api/services/system_health_service.py`, `tests/test_api_chatbot.py`
   - Also found, **not** fixed (outside this repo or outside scope): (a) **87 × `GET /auth/me` 401** in 3h — the browser session token is missing or expired, which on its own makes the whole terminal look dead; (b) **`GET /system/health` is admin-only** (`require_admin`), so every non-admin user gets 401 and the SYSTEM tab can only ever render as unknown/offline for them — 7 of the 9 configured users are role `user`; (c) the Nuxt UI calls **`GET /portfolio/nav`** and **`GET /signals/reports/portfolio-risk/latest`** without the required `book_id`, producing repeated **422**s; (d) the 30s GET cache on job polling in `MindwealthUI_Vue` is still unfixed, so the 503 banner can still recur.
   - Prod impact: **yes** — `api/services/chatbot_service.py` and `api/services/system_health_service.py` must merge `chatbot-dev` → `chatbot-prod`. Prod runs the old history endpoint; it does not 500 there only because prod also still has the history-corruption bug that hides it. **Merging the entry 7 durability fix without this one would turn prod's silent history wipe into a hard 500.** Recorded in `dev_to_prod_migration_todos.md`.

16. **Rohit: "report is dated / not updated daily", "Tavily not working — neither chatbot nor macro nightly briefing", "SYSTEM tab shows services offline"** — SUCCESSFUL
   - Summary: Four separate causes, none of them Tavily. **(1) Dead Claude model id — the big one.** Every Claude call *outside* the chatbot was returning `404 not_found_error: model: claude-sonnet-4-20250514`, a retired id. `generate_nightly_briefing()` catches the exception and falls back to `_template_briefing()`, so **the nightly macro narrative on the site has been template text, not Claude**, and the Tavily headlines gathered for that prompt were discarded along with the call. The same dead id was the default in `src/conviction_engine/agent_dims.py` and `api/services/analyst_copy_service.py`, and was pinned in `macro_intelligence/CONFIG.yaml` (both `model` and `geo_model`) **and in `.env` as `MACRO_CLAUDE_MODEL`**. All moved to `claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929`, the id the chatbot already runs against successfully. Rohit's instinct ("neither the chatbot instant nor the macro nightly briefing") was right about the symptom; the cause was the model id, not Tavily. **(2) "A CPI release is pending this week" was permanently true.** `_pending_cpi_release()` scanned the **trailing** 7 days and ignored whether an `actual` had landed, so it stayed true for a week after every print — and never actually reset, because `bls_pull.try_bls_cpi_pull()` writes an observation row stamped `release_date = today` on **every** nightly run, guaranteeing a row inside that trailing window. Window is now strictly forward. **(3) The SYSTEM tab rows are hardcoded in the frontend.** `MindwealthUI_Vue/server/utils/overwatch-panel.ts:141-155` returns `India CSV pipeline`, `Claude API` and `Tavily` as literal `status:'warn', detail:'Could not fetch from server'` constants — the UI never calls `GET /system/health` at all. Those three rows have never reflected reality. Real status, measured live: **Tavily `ok`**, **Claude API `ok` (267ms)**, Sheets `ok`, Macro `ok`, SSI `ok`. **(4)** The "date didn't update" complaint is the already-logged stale `data_updated_at` (entries 11/13); the US CSV row and Google Sheets row on that tab both read from that same meta, which is why the screenshot showed `5607m ago` and `2026-08-14`.
   - Verification: `call_claude()` returns a real completion again (was 404). Regenerated the 2026-08-17 briefing — **7,233 chars of genuine Claude analysis** replacing the template, and it now reports the CPI surprise as **−0.026pp, "not hot"** instead of announcing a pending release. `GET /macro/runic/nightly` on `:8507` serves it with `pending_cpi_release: false`. New `tests/test_pending_cpi_release.py` (3 cases: a printed CPI does not stay pending, the nightly's own observation row does not count, a genuine upcoming release inside 7 days does). `pytest tests/ -q` → **814 passed, 1 failed, 3 skipped** (same known Monday-only failure). Dev API restarted, `smoke-test-apis.sh` **11/11 PASS**, prod isolation intact.
   - Files changed: `src/macro_intelligence/output/json_writer.py`, `src/macro_intelligence/claude/_client.py`, `src/conviction_engine/agent_dims.py`, `api/services/analyst_copy_service.py`, `macro_intelligence/CONFIG.yaml`, `.env.example`, `tests/test_pending_cpi_release.py` (new); **`.env` edited on dev (not in git)**
   - Deliberately **not** fixed: `bls_pull.try_bls_cpi_pull()` stamping `release_date = datetime.now()`. That makes `pending_releases` mean "the day the nightly ran" rather than the real release date, and it also feeds `fetch_cpi_surprise_series()` and `pre_catalyst.upcoming_event` (which is why the fragility panel shows a CPI catalyst at `days_to_event 0` every single day). Changing what a `pending_releases` row means needs Rohit's sign-off, not a unilateral patch. Evidence: identical `actual=0.0737` rows dated 08-10, 08-11, 08-13, 08-14 and 08-17, each `created_at` 18:00 on that day.
   - Prod impact: **yes** — all 6 tracked files merge `chatbot-dev` → `chatbot-prod`, **plus a `.env` edit on prod** (`MACRO_CLAUDE_MODEL`), without which prod keeps serving template narratives. Prod has been producing template-only briefings for as long as the model id has been retired.

17. **"Could not reach the analyst" — root cause, permanent fix across both repos, live replay of all five query types** — SUCCESSFUL
   - Live replay first (dev `:8507`, real Claude calls, full create-session → POST → poll → history round trip): **Q1** the original NZ replacement question now routes **HYBRID** (was `WEB_RAG`) with `Conviction Overlay` in the flow trace and quotes `Signal Quality Composite Score -3.7 (Tier C)`, conviction 7.5 TACTICAL BUY 75% sizing, P/E 27.71, ROIC 12%, BQ 7.5/15 — 60s. **Q2** the 503 chat returns INTERNAL + overlay, top pick `MAZDOCK.NS` score **79.5** with its own engine disagreeing (`CANCEL BUY, BQ 6, valuation tax −7`) — 55s. **Q5** exits: INTERNAL + overlay, real positions — 70s. **Q3** AAPL deep dive works (35s) but gets **no** conviction block — the wording misses `_CONVICTION_RELEVANT_RE`. **Q4** "current macro regime / which combo is dominant" still routes **`WEB_RAG`** and answers "transitional, mixed signals" from the web while our own Runic engine says Combo F dominant, week 20 of 26, TACTICAL EASY MONEY — the same defect class as the original complaint, for macro wording, and the chatbot has no runic feed at all. History endpoint 200 on all five; no 503, no timeouts.
   - **Root cause of the error banner — four faults, all in `MindwealthUI_Vue`, none of them an unreachable backend.** (1) `mindwealthFetch` cached **every** GET for 30s including job polls, so a 2.5s poll loop was replayed a stale `running` body for 30s and a job completing at 147s still read as running at the 150s deadline. (2) The cache key was `method:path` with **no caller identity** — two signed-in users hitting the same user-scoped path within 30s could be served each other's response. (3) `pollChatJob` abandoned the whole answer on a **single** `null`, and `mindwealthFetch` returns `null` for *any* failure — one 502, one API restart, one dropped socket. (4) Budget exhaustion threw a **503 and discarded the job id**, so a live answer became unreachable, and holding one HTTP request open for the full 330s backend budget exposed the chat to every intermediate timeout in the path. Plus a fifth, in **our** repo: jobs run in worker threads, so a restart killed the thread but left the record saying `running` **forever** — every deploy stranded in-flight answers and the client polled to its own budget before showing the banner.
   - Fixes: paths under `/chatbot` are never cached; cache key carries a non-reversible bearer-token fingerprint; the poll tolerates 8 consecutive failures and reports a completed-but-empty job immediately instead of looping to timeout; `POST /api/chat` hands back `{pending: true, job_id}` after **45s** and the browser continues on a new `GET /api/chat/job/<id>` route (420s budget, job id persisted to localStorage so a refresh mid-answer does not strand it); the blanket catch now distinguishes 503 / 401-403 / network; the session id is persisted **before** the answer arrives so a mid-flight failure cannot orphan a session; and `JobStore.fail_orphaned()` runs once in the API lifespan to fail anything left `queued`/`running` by a dead process.
   - **A test caught a bug in the fix itself:** the first cache-bypass pattern was anchored `^/chatbot/` but `mindwealthFetch` matches against the *normalized* path, which already carries `/api/v1`. The API log still showed 30s poll spacing (3 polls). Corrected to an unanchored pattern and re-verified: **24 polls at ~2.5s** across the same window.
   - Verification: end to end through the **Nuxt BFF on `:8514`** with a real `mw_access_token` cookie — NZ question returns `pending` at 45.1s, one resume poll, **5,501-char answer at 60.1s, no 503**; the signals+quality question **7,647 chars at 57.8s**; a short AAPL question returns **inline at 25.1s** with no handoff. `pytest tests/` → **817 passed, 4 skipped, 0 failed** (the Monday-only signals-surface failure passes now that the calendar has rolled). `npm run build` clean, 3.96 MB. Dev API and dev Nuxt both restarted; `smoke-test-apis.sh` **11/11 PASS**, prod isolation intact.
   - Files changed: **`MindWealth_UI`** — `api/jobs/store.py`, `api/main.py`, `tests/test_api_chatbot.py` (2 new cases). **`MindwealthUI_Vue`** (`ui-dev`) — `server/utils/mindwealth-client.ts`, `server/utils/mindwealth-data.ts`, `server/api/chat.post.ts`, `server/api/chat/job/[jobId].get.ts` (new), `composables/useClaudePanel.ts`, `types/api.ts`
   - Still open, reported not fixed: **Q4 macro questions route to the web** and contradict our own engine — needs macro/regime/combo wording in the override **plus** a new runic feed (`/macro/runic/nightly`, `/macro/regime`, `/macro/ssi/summary`), i.e. a new data source, not a regex; **Q3** per-ticker questions get no conviction block; the SYSTEM tab still hardcodes Tavily/Claude/India rows; history-restore-on-refresh (`useClaudePanel.ts:82`) untouched.
   - Prod impact: **yes, both repos.** `MindWealth_UI` merge `chatbot-dev` → `chatbot-prod`; `MindwealthUI_Vue` merge `ui-dev` → prod UI branch **plus `npm run build` and a service restart on the prod host**. The two must ship together — the client-side resume is what makes the backend's longer answer budget safe.

18. **Chatbot audit — 290 stored sessions mined for real usage, 13 representative queries replayed through the UI, gaps catalogued** — SUCCESSFUL (investigation; no code changes)
   - Method: extracted **169 user messages across 290 stored sessions**, clustered them, then replayed 13 queries in the dominant shapes through the **Nuxt BFF on `:8514`** with a real session cookie — ticker lookup, MTM math, ranked screening, signals+news blend, cross-ticker comparison, recommendation, historical deep dive, platform-meta, multi-turn follow-up, and two adversarial prompts found in the real history.
   - **Working:** every query returned, none 503'd, none timed out. Ticker lookups 12–25s, ranked screens 35–43s, a 15,817-char deep dive over 109 rows completed at **125s** via the new resume path. Multi-turn continuity resolves pronouns correctly ("now calculate the mtm according to the last trade" → AAPL FRACTAL TRACK from session context). History endpoint 200 with the right message count. Hybrid answers reconcile internal vs web prices explicitly (TSLA $339.30 internal vs $343.75 web, both labelled). **Both adversarial prompts were refused cleanly** — no system prompt, no API keys, no path disclosure.
   - **Gap 1 (data, user-visible):** MTM is anchored on `Signal Open Price` while the answer presents `Signal Date/Price` as the entry. SOXX SIGMASHELL: entry shown as **$507.68**, current **$559.12** — the arithmetic a user does is **+10.13%**, but the platform reports **+13.55%** because it divides by the open of **$492.42**. Same family as the previously logged 5A defect. The chatbot faithfully quotes the field and then labels it "above entry", which makes the inconsistency look like a chatbot error.
   - **Gap 2 (data):** `chatbot/data/entry.csv` holds **25,709 rows but only 2,671 unique (symbol, function)** pairs — ~90% duplication. Ranked screens fetch 100 rows, so effective breadth is far narrower than the row count suggests.
   - **Gap 3 (response):** the bot recomputes MTM instead of quoting the stored field — answered AAPL **−0.11%** where the data says **−0.14%**. Small, but it means chat and site disagree.
   - **Gap 4 (response):** ranked screens present `Sharpe 7.88` / `win rate 100.0%` as "exceptional risk-adjusted returns" without the sample size sitting in the same field (`100.0%, Past 5 years, 14`). No minimum-n guard and no scepticism about a 100% win rate.
   - **Gap 5 (router/vocabulary):** "give me a short summary about claude report" → **CONVERSATIONAL**, and the bot asked whether "Claude" was a *stock ticker*. `claude_report` is a first-class signal type with a live 112 KB `chatbot/data/claude_report.txt` refreshed daily, and the deep-dive query pulled it successfully. The bot does not know its own vocabulary.
   - **Gap 6 (router/meta):** "What signal types exist?" returned a generic LONG/SHORT/entry/exit textbook answer instead of the platform's actual taxonomy (`entry`, `exit`, `portfolio_target_achieved`, `breadth`, `claude_report`) or its functions (FRACTAL TRACK, TRENDPULSE, DELTADRIFT, SIGMASHELL, PULSEGAUGE, BASELINEDIVERGENCE). `GET /chatbot/signal-types` already serves the right list.
   - **Gap 7 (web):** stale web quotes are surfaced beside live ones without prominence — an NVDA quote dated **2026-07-18** appeared next to today's price in the same comparison block.
   - **Gap 8 (data surface):** the chatbot reaches signals (entry/exit/target/breadth/claude_report) + conviction over HTTP, but has **no macro/runic, no SSI/sentiment, and no portfolio/NAV/sizing feed** — which is why macro questions still answer from the web.
   - **Historical defects found in the stored sessions:** a `NameError: name 'truncated_latest_user' is not defined` (since fixed in code) was **fed back into the pipeline as the next user query** — the engine web-searched its own error message and paid for an LLM turn on it; and raw Anthropic billing errors including `request_id` were rendered to the user as chat replies.
   - Prod impact: none (investigation only). Findings feed the pending list in `dev_to_prod_migration_todos.md`.

17. **Topbar showing "Aug 13, 08:00 PM EDT" — Nuxt stamped report dates at midnight UTC (repo `MindwealthUI_Vue`, branch `ui-dev`)** — SUCCESSFUL
   - Summary: User reported the site still reading `Aug 13, 08:00 PM EDT`. **This is a different defect from entries 11/13** — the backend was correct. `GET /api/v1/meta` on both `:8507` and `:8506` returns `2026-08-14T16:00:00-04:00`, and `composables/useAppMeta.ts` correctly renders `data_updated_at.datetime` in `America/New_York`. The bad value was manufactured in Nitro: `server/utils/mindwealth-data.ts` `metaFromSource()` parsed the date out of the report filename and stamped `datetime: <date>T00:00:00Z` with `timezone: 'UTC'`. Midnight UTC on 2026-08-14 **is** 8:00 PM EDT on 2026-08-13, so the topbar rendered the previous evening — an exact match for the reported string (verified: `new Date('2026-08-14T00:00:00Z')` formats to `Aug 13, 08:00 PM EDT`; the market-close stamp formats to `Aug 14, 04:00 PM EDT`). Two further sites did the same thing — the signals-list meta (`reportDate`) and the shortlist meta (`report.report_date`). The correct DST-safe helper `buildMarketCloseDataUpdatedAt()` already existed in `server/utils/data-updated-at.ts` but only `sentiment-mapper.ts` used it. Also: `loadMeta()` never called the backend `/meta` at all — it derived everything from the overlay filename — so the API's authoritative timestamp was unused.
   - Fix: all three sites now use `buildMarketCloseDataUpdatedAt()` (16:00 America/New_York, DST-safe offset), and `loadMeta()` now calls `mindwealthFetch<ApiMeta>('/meta')` first and merges via `mergeApiMeta()`, falling back to filename derivation only when the endpoint is unavailable. `metaFromSource()` feeds 11 call sites, so every page's "as of" is corrected, not just the topbar.
   - Verification: `rg "T00:00:00Z"` across `server/ components/ pages/ composables/ utils/` → **no matches left**. `npm run build` clean (Node 20, 3.95 MB output); `mindwealth-ui-dev` restarted, **active**, `:8514` returns 200. Render proof run in Node against both stamps (old → `Aug 13, 08:00 PM EDT`, new → `Aug 14, 04:00 PM EDT`). **End-to-end BFF proof obtained:** `GET :8514/api/meta` now returns `datetime: 2026-08-14T16:00:00-04:00`, `data_source: live`, `market_label: MindWealth API v1.10.8` and both `source_files` entries — i.e. the browser will render `Aug 14, 04:00 PM EDT`.
   - **Robust test + dev deploy (skill run, 2026-08-17):** branches verified (`chatbot-dev`, `ui-dev`). No Python file changed, so the API surface was re-checked rather than re-implemented: `pytest tests/test_api_*.py -q` → **187 passed, 1 failed** (the known Monday-only `test_shortlist_mtm_not_stale_zero_for_aged_signals`); the full `pytest tests/ -q` earlier this session was **810 passed / 1 failed / 3 skipped**. `smoke-test-apis.sh` **all PASS** (dev :8507 v1.10.8, prod :8506 v1.8.1, `conviction_store` paths isolated and writable; **neither prod service restarted**). `journalctl -u mindwealth-ui-dev` clean after restart (`Listening on http://0.0.0.0:8514`). Steps 2–3 not applicable: no FastAPI route, schema or response-shape change, so no OpenAPI export and no docs-submodule commit. Mock audit: no mock path is reachable — `resolveApiData()` only serves `getMockMeta()` when `NUXT_USE_MOCK_DATA=true`, and neither `mindwealth-ui-dev` nor `mindwealth-ui` sets it (`systemctl show -p Environment`), so a backend outage degrades to `unavailable`, not to the mock 2026-05-12 payload.
   - **Two findings surfaced during verification, neither caused by this change:**
     - **Prod UI is still serving the bug.** `GET :8512/api/meta` returns `2026-08-14T00:00:00Z` — the old bundle is still resident in the running process, so `www.mindwealth.co` shows `Aug 13, 08:00 PM EDT` until `mindwealth-ui` restarts. Not restarted: that is a public deploy and needs the user's go-ahead.
     - **BFF auth accepts any non-empty cookie.** `requireAuth()` (`server/utils/require-auth.ts`) checks only that `mw_access_token` *exists*, never that it is valid, and `mindwealth-client.ts` authenticates upstream with the server-side `NUXT_API_KEY` rather than the user's token. A fabricated cookie returns live data (that is how the BFF proof above was obtained). Pre-existing, affects `:8512` equally, raised as a separate follow-up task.
   - Files changed: `MindwealthUI_Vue/server/utils/mindwealth-data.ts` (separate repo). Pushed `ui-dev` @ **`fe7ebf1`** to `D-ParthChauhan/MindwealthUI_Vue`, author `divsum127`.
   - **Note on today's date:** with this fix the topbar reads `Aug 14, 04:00 PM EDT` — still the **last trading day**, which is correct until the 22:00 UTC pull lands Monday's file. The date being "behind" over a weekend is entry 11's root cause B and remains unaddressed by design (no "as of last trading day" label).
   - Prod impact: **yes, and partly already applied.** Both `mindwealth-ui-dev` (`:8514`) and `mindwealth-ui` (`:8512`, public `www.mindwealth.co`) run from the **same tree and the same `.output`**, so `npm run build` has already replaced the bundle prod serves; `:8512` keeps running the old code only until its next restart, at which point it picks this up without any merge. Recorded in `dev_to_prod_migration_todos.md`.

19. **Root-cause fix for all 8 findings from the sheet-reply truth audit (W1-W7)** — SUCCESSFUL
   - Summary: Fixed the eight audit findings at source rather than patching symptoms. Six of the eight shared one root cause: the SSI pull layer had no resilience contract — 30+ bare `except` blocks returning an empty Series with no log, `yahoo_pull.fetch_yahoo_close` returning empty on failure without raising, four Yahoo-derived inputs with no disk cache while every other input had one, and no `logging` import anywhere in `run_ssi_daily.py` / `daily_run.py` / `positioning.py`. **State at start of work (2026-08-18): Layer 2 running 2 of 6, Layer 3 3 of 4, SSI 0.3064, L2 UNCONFIRMED, size multiplier 1.2× → 0.8× on missing data alone, job exit 0. State after: Layer 2 6/6, Layer 3 4/4, Layer 1 3/4 (NAAIM only), multiplier 1.2× CONFIRMED, degradation logged and gated.**
   - **W1 — pull resilience.** New `src/sentiment_superindex/data/yahoo_cache.py` (per-ticker CSV cache with `source` provenance, reusing the existing `scraper_utils` load/merge/save trio and the CNN F&G provenance convention) and `pull_guard.py` (`log_pull_failure` / `log_pull_empty` writing to both `logging` and the existing `data_pull_log`). **New `cboe_indices.py`: CBOE's own free daily history files are now the primary source for ^VIX / ^VIX3M / ^SKEW.** Yahoo's `^VIX3M` is dead past 2026-07-17 and its documented `^VXV` fallback is delisted — CBOE serves the same series current same-day (verified VIX 9,252 rows to 2026-08-17, VIX3M 4,253, SKEW 9,207). Yahoo stays as fallback and the cache unions both, preserving the pre-2009 VIX3M history CBOE lacks. Bare excepts in `naaim_pull`, `put_call_pull`, `cnn_fear_greed`, `sp500_breadth`, `margin_debt_pull` now log without changing control flow. `retry_cache.pull_with_cache` treats an empty result as failure (it only checked `None`, so a dead source logged `OK` with zero rows).
   - **W2 — coverage gate.** New `coverage:` block in `SSI_CONFIG.yaml` + `config.coverage_policy()`. `_layer_signal_coverage` now returns `reliable` / `unreliable_reason` / `nominal_weight_retained`; `_build_layer` suppresses the score below threshold; `positioning.py` forces `ssi_multiplier = 1.0` and `layer2_status = COVERAGE_INCOMPLETE` when any layer is unreliable. Gate is **weight-aware, not just count-aware** (Layer 1's inputs are not interchangeable — NAAIM alone is 0.35). `daily_run.log_coverage` reports per-layer state and `run_ssi_daily.py` exits 2 when degraded (`--allow-degraded` to override). **Verified the gate drops zero historical dates** that were not already dropped by pre-existing behaviour, so the 5y percentile baseline is unchanged.
   - **W3 — CFTC.** `_download_frames` now checks the local zip set is *complete* for the requested window and downloads only the gaps, instead of returning early whenever any local file existed — this is the exact cause of the prod-vs-dev percentile split (prod ranked against ~31 weekly prints, 27/31 = 87.1%, vs dev's 82/155 = 52.9%). **Prod self-heals on first run after deploy; no manual data-cache edit needed.** `_rolling_pctile` refuses to publish a rank below `MIN_PCTILE_WINDOW_OBS = 100`. `_TFF_RAW_CACHE` keyed by `start_year` (was a single global in a long-lived API process). `refresh_cftc_zip_if_stale()` now called from the SSI path, closing the Fri-15:30-ET → Mon blind spot. `Report_Date_as_YYYY-MM-DD` parsed with an explicit format, removing the pandas UserWarning flood that was the only content in `ssi_daily.log`.
   - **W4 — one source of truth.** Deleted the Python literals in `config.py`; `staleness_policy()` now raises if the YAML block is missing rather than scoring on a code default. Resolved policy published in the API payload (`staleness_policy`) and the Nuxt util reads it instead of its hardcoded "mirrors SSI_CONFIG.yaml" copy. Tests rewritten to compare the resolved policy against the YAML file itself. Decay study reads config. Orphan `MAX_FFORWARD_FILL_*_DAYS = 5` documented as deprecated and explicitly decoupled. Stale pre-calibration git worktree `.claude/worktrees/adoring-chatelet-bafe90` removed. **Confirmed the live values are Rohit's own instruction (sheet C46: "suggest weekly 8, daily 3, monthly 30"), shipped by Test 21 on 2026-08-07 — the 10/1/25 figures quoted in the sheet reply were never what ran.**
   - **W5 — regime feed.** `update_regime_v2_to_date()` wired into `run_friday_pull`; the table had **no scheduled writer at all** (only the manual `run_all.py`), which is why it froze at 2026-06-05 while the endpoint served it as current. Gap backfilled — now current to 2026-08-14, 19 Fridays, zero failures. Silent `except: continue` replaced with counted logging. Feed now carries `vix_mult` / `trend_mult` / `hy_mult` / `chain_mult` by joining the **existing** `four_book_engine.load_full_ceiling_chain_series` (mirrors the live `_compute_ceiling` thresholds, so history and live agree by construction) with `overlay_history_start` (1996-12-31) and `latest_evaluation_date` surfaced so coverage cannot be assumed. `regime_block._trend_regime_label` reads the cached ^GSPC series instead of a live uncached `yf.Ticker().history()`.
   - **W6 — Nuxt (`MindwealthUI_Vue`, `ui-dev`).** Layer 2 gated rows display the **z-score the badge is computed from**, with the raw print moved to the sub-line (`LAYER2_NORM_GATED_KEYS` existed with a comment saying exactly this but was only consulted by the sub-line builder). Duplicate `z` token removed at source. **`buildSentimentBanner` had been computing a CFTC squeeze/liquidity-exit banner since it was written and no component ever consumed `data.banner`** — now mounted via new `SentimentBannerStrip.vue` and extended with two data-quality banners (coverage incomplete, mixed as-of dates). COT "next release" no longer printed when past — and `next_release` itself was being computed as *expected_release + 3 days*, which lands on a Friday already gone (`next release Fri 14 Aug` shown on 18 Aug); now rolls forward. CFTC raw rows carry freshness. New `formatOrdinal` helper kills `83th`/`53th`. Stale hardcoded `2026-05-12` `source_files` no longer leak into the sentiment response. Dead `pct_above_200dma` entry removed from `LAYER1_LABELS`.
   - **W7 — guards.** New `tests/test_ssi_feed_health.py` (14 tests) and `test/sentiment-mapper.spec.ts` (9 tests), each naming the defect it prevents. `export_data_validation.py` extended to all 14 scored SSI inputs with per-input age vs cap (the previous report covered only the 9 declared in `DATA_SOURCES.yaml`, part of why a dead ^VIX3M went unnoticed) — **also fixed a pre-existing crash in that script** (`float()` on a multi-column row) that meant it could not run at all. Overwatch alert raised when a layer is below coverage minimum.
   - **Two unrelated live bugs surfaced by the new logging**, both previously invisible: FRED series `BOGZFL224066003Q` returns **404** for margin debt (not in any layer, so no scoring impact), and `export_data_validation.py` had been broken.
   - Verification: `pytest tests/` (see entry note), 44 SSI-specific tests pass, `npm run test` 47 pass, `npm run typecheck` 45 errors vs a 46 pre-existing baseline (no new errors), dev API `:8507` and dev Nuxt `:8514` both rebuilt and verified against the live BFF payload.
   - Files changed: `src/sentiment_superindex/{config.py,data/{yahoo_cache.py,pull_guard.py,cboe_indices.py,yahoo_inputs.py,naaim_pull.py,put_call_pull.py,cnn_fear_greed.py,sp500_breadth.py,margin_debt_pull.py,cftc_patterns.py,pull_all.py,alignment.py},engine/{superindex.py,positioning.py,regime_block.py},jobs/daily_run.py,analysis/staleness_decay_study.py}`, `src/macro_intelligence/{data/{cftc_pull.py,retry_cache.py},output/regime_feed_export.py,jobs/friday_pull.py,analysis/regime_experiments/shadow_backfill.py}`, `src/portfolio_nav/four_book_engine.py`, `api/{routers/macro.py,services/{reports_service.py,macro_service.py,analyst_service.py}}`, `scripts/{run_ssi_daily.py,export_data_validation.py}`, `macro_intelligence/SSI_CONFIG.yaml`, `tests/{test_ssi_feed_health.py,test_ssi_staleness.py,test_ssi_vix_ratio.py,test_series_alignment.py}`, and in `MindwealthUI_Vue`: `utils/{ordinal.ts,signal-freshness.ts}`, `server/utils/sentiment-mapper.ts`, `components/sentiment/SentimentBannerStrip.vue`, `pages/sentiment.vue`, `types/api.ts`, `test/sentiment-mapper.spec.ts`.
   - **NAAIM remains open and is not a code problem.** The public feed is gone: naaim.org dropped the table, `index.naaim.org` is now a sign-in page, both public iframes are frozen at 2026-05-13 (older than our own cache), Wayback has no snapshot after June 2026, the WordPress REST API exposes only monthly newsletters, and FRED carries no NAAIM series. The scraper was still repaired (it now parses the iframe — 131 rows vs 0 before — and resumes automatically if NAAIM unfreezes it) and the absence is now loud rather than silent. NAAIM is 0.35 of Layer 1, so restoring it needs a product decision from Rohit: membership, manual weekly entry (a `manual` provenance tag is reserved for it), or re-specced Layer 1 weights.

3. **Investigate: dev API `:8507` slow query performance** — SUCCESSFUL (investigation only, no code changes)
   - Summary: Live-benchmarked dev (`:8507`) vs prod (`:8506`) on host `51.20.53.218`. Service healthy; most endpoints similar sequentially, but parallel dashboard fan-out ~1.4s (dev) vs ~0.75s (prod). **Primary code bottleneck:** `GET /api/v1/conviction/alerts/daily` runs `run_daily_universe()` → `daily_update()` + `save_record()` for all ~195 tickers on every request (~0.6–0.8s, 195 disk writes). **Secondary:** `GET /portfolio/sizer` hits yfinance for SPX 200d MA each call (~0.15–0.5s); cold imports after `--reload` add ~1s. **Host contention:** `send_email.py uo` at ~100% CPU for 2+ hours on 4-core box. **Dev config:** `mindwealth-api-dev.service` runs uvicorn with `--reload` (12k+ file watcher, single worker, cache wiped on reload). **UI pattern:** dashboard fires 20–30 parallel requests incl. N+1 `/conviction/tickers/{symbol}`; logged-in users can hit rate-limit 429 on `POST /signals/check-degradation`. Recommended fixes ranked: stop CPU hog, serve cached daily alerts (not full recompute), TTL-cache SPX trend mult, drop `--reload` when not editing, add workers / fix N+1 frontend.
   - Files inspected (no code changes): `api/services/conviction_service.py`, `src/conviction_engine/engine.py`, `api/services/portfolio_service.py`, `scripts/mindwealth-api-dev.service`, `config/rate_limits.yaml`, systemd/journal logs

### 2026-08-02

1. **Investigate: Divyanshu — LIQUIDITY EXIT (RM 3yr pct &lt;30 AND FM 3yr pct &gt;60) not shown on Sentiment/Layer 3 panel or Overwatch despite condition being met (RM=28th, FM=93rd today)** — SUCCESSFUL (investigation only, no code changes)
   - Summary: Confirmed the underlying RM/FM percentile numbers on the dashboard are real and correctly computed — `persist_cftc_snapshot()` (`src/macro_intelligence/data/cftc_pull.py:333-362`) pulls CFTC.gov TFF data, splits it into RM = "Asset Mgr" net (`parse_cftc_rm_dataframe`) and FM = "Lev Money/Leveraged Funds" net (`parse_cftc_dataframe`), and computes a rolling percentile over `pctile_window_weeks: 156` (= 3 years, `macro_intelligence/CONFIG.yaml:319-328`) via `_rolling_pctile()`. These are exposed as raw display numbers only, via `cftc_layer3_snapshot()` → `layer3_for_date()` → `build_positioning_payload()`'s `inputs.layer3_cftc.{fm_pctile,rm_pctile}` (`src/sentiment_superindex/engine/positioning.py:98-123`), served by `GET /api/v1/analytics/sentiment/layers` (documented as "Primary endpoint for Alpha Terminal SSI / sentiment layer UI" — `docs/mindwealth-api-docs/services/analytics/endpoints/get-sentiment-layers.md`). **Root cause of the missing flag: the "RM&lt;30 AND FM&gt;60 → LIQUIDITY EXIT" rule was never coded as a live check anywhere in the app — it exists only as an offline backtest/research script.** It is Test 4 of the SSI validation suite (`src/sentiment_superindex/analysis/cftc_grid.py::run_liquidity_exit_grid()`, artifacts in `docs/ssi_validation/04_liquidity_exit_grid.md` + `macro_intelligence/analysis/ssi_validation/04_liquidity_exit_grid_*.json`), and was **explicitly signed off as "Macro flag only — not an SSI gate"** in `docs/ssi_validation/SIGNOFF.md` (Test 4 row) and `docs/ssi_validation/SSI_THRESHOLD_JUSTIFICATION.md` Part D2 ("In SSI production? No — macro combo research. Status: APPROVED as macro research" — same language for the sibling Test 3 SQUEEZE grid). Verified the two places a flag *could* have surfaced and confirmed neither evaluates this condition: (1) `superindex.py::build_layer3()` only ever z-scores the raw `cftc_fm_net`/`cftc_rm_net` values into a single Layer 3 composite score (25% SSI weight) — no percentile-threshold branch exists there; (2) the named-combo engine (`src/macro_intelligence/engine/combo_detector.py::detect_named_combos()`, Combos A–G) and the Overwatch sentiment-banner builder (`api/services/analyst_service.py::_build_sentiment_warning_alerts()`, lines ~344-378) only react to aggregate `ssi_level`/`posture`/`layer2_status`/`long_signal_active`/`short_signal_active` — none of the 7 named combos encodes an RM/FM percentile pattern, so there is no code path that could ever raise a Liquidity Exit banner or Overwatch alert today. Conclusion given to Divyanshu: not a bug in the computation (RM 28th/FM 93rd match the correct 3yr-window math) and not silently broken wiring — it's a deliberate, documented product decision from the SSI validation sign-off to keep this pattern as research-only, which is why it never got a UI flag or Overwatch hook. Recommended next step (not implemented, needs a decision): promote it to a named combo/flag (e.g. new Combo H in `combo_detector.py` + a `liquidity_exit` field in `build_positioning_payload()` + a branch in `_build_sentiment_warning_alerts()`), using the SIGNOFF.md-documented thresholds (RM&lt;15–40, FM&gt;45–75, empirically n≈96–123) as the calibration starting point — needs Divyanshu/Rohit to pick a specific RM/FM cutoff pair first, same open item as the SQUEEZE grid (D1) and the Percentile-SSI deployment (Test 9) already awaiting sign-off in that file.
   - Files inspected (no code changes): `src/macro_intelligence/data/cftc_pull.py`, `src/sentiment_superindex/data/cftc_ssi.py`, `src/sentiment_superindex/data/pull_all.py`, `src/sentiment_superindex/engine/positioning.py`, `src/sentiment_superindex/engine/superindex.py`, `src/macro_intelligence/engine/combo_detector.py`, `api/services/analyst_service.py`, `api/services/overwatch_event_bus.py`, `api/routers/analytics.py`, `src/sentiment_superindex/analysis/cftc_grid.py`, `macro_intelligence/CONFIG.yaml`, `macro_intelligence/SSI_CONFIG.yaml`, `docs/ssi_validation/SIGNOFF.md`, `docs/ssi_validation/SSI_THRESHOLD_JUSTIFICATION.md`, `docs/ssi_validation/04_liquidity_exit_grid.md`
   - Prod impact: none (investigation only). If a fix is approved later, it touches `chatbot-dev` first per normal workflow.

   - Summary: Implemented all 22 items from `.cursor/plans/conviction_engine_fixes_v2_62be381f.plan.md` (built from the 28 July consolidated note + Rohit's 30 July reply to the 11 open questions + the FS-slice follow-up doc). Highlights: (1) added `bank` and `high_margin_hardware` as calibrated business types (6 total) with a new `bank_valuation.py` module (efficiency-ratio margin quality, equity/assets balance sheet, P/TBV-vs-ROE excess-return valuation tax — **supersedes** the simpler flat Price/Book table Rohit gave in his Q1 answer, per the follow-up doc's explicit correction) and an EV/forward-EBITDA valuation driver for hardware; (2) added `COVERAGE INCOMPLETE` as a third hard gate (distinct from `CANCEL BUY`) for any business type outside the 6 calibrated ones (insurers, deep-value, etc.), wired into `verdict_for_buy`/`verdict_for_sell` and the API schema; (3) fixed two real bugs — `growth_multiple_fragility`'s condition was inverted vs spec, and the universal 4x-EV/rev/-5 valuation-tax floor was previously a per-type trigger list instead of a universal rule (hardware exempt); (4) rebuilt `calculate_fs_score()` from an ad hoc formula into a fully specced, symmetric PE-percentile/OEY/EV-fwd-rev point-table `fs_score_breakdown()` — this also **fixes the CRM live-dashboard verdict bug** (displayed `CANCEL BUY` vs a drawer showing BQ+8/tax-5→+3) by making `fs_score`/`fs_class`/`fs_cap_breakdown` always derive from one atomic computation inside `daily_update()`, never three independently-settable fields; (5) added standalone tiered buyback-suspension and dividend-cut penalty flags (0/-1/-2/-3 by decline-%  tier, combined -4 floor) plus a >10%-analyst-estimate revenue-miss recalculation trigger; (6) built `adjusted_eps.py` (company's own trailing effective tax rate, not a flat 21/25%, with a documented pretax-income-negative fallback) and a materiality-gated (>5% of trailing NI) adjusted-PE substitution into the percentile calc; (7) built a Tier 2/3 non-US PE-history fallback (`reconstruct_quarterly_eps_from_net_income()` in `pe_history_core.py`) for filers with no direct EPS row in `quarterly_income_stmt`, explicitly skipping the already-confirmed-dead Macrotrends tier; (8) made `market_yield_threshold()` return an explicit `None` for KR/JP/CN tickers instead of silently defaulting to the US threshold; (9) built a cheap classification-only universe diff pass (`classify_universe_diff()`/`run_universe_classification_pass()` in `fundamentals.py` + a new CLI script) that only queues tickers for a full `full_recalculation()` when they flip into `bank`/`high_margin_hardware`/`coverage_incomplete`; (10) carried forward the original note's deal-delay agent, TAM three-tier sourcing (SEC XBRL backlog as Tier 1 in new `tam_sourcing.py`, agentic Tier 2/3), and G2-source-exclusion-for-hardware fix, plus full sourcing/confidence/evidence transparency in the Streamlit scorecard; (11) exposed `fs_cap_breakdown`/`yield_trap_breakdown` on every record (already returned by the existing `GET /conviction/tickers/{ticker}` dict endpoint) for Parth's Vue Engine Layers click-through UI. Every decision, correction, and deferred/out-of-scope item (insurers/REIT/biotech, multi-segment tie-breaks, non-US CEO-tenure/insider-ownership sourcing "Tavela") is logged in new `instruction_docs/conviction_engine_issues/conviction_fixes_decisions.md`.    - Summary: Rohit flagged that a GOOG/NVDA "recent entry/exit levels" chatbot answer included a "Resistance Levels Context — Web Context [Source 1,2,3]" section with generic internet TA (death cross, Fibonacci, resistance below current price) instead of using MindWealth's own Targets/Stop Loss ladders. Added 13 new unit tests (`tests/test_llm_router_guardrails.py`, incl. subtests) covering the override, the non-override cases (pure news query, genuine hybrid query), and both regex copies; full suite (`tests/` — 654 passed, 2 skipped, no regressions) run via `.venv/bin/pytest`. ### 2026-07-29

1. **Investigate: New Signals page shows two identical-looking cards (^GSPC / BASE / Weekly / Long / same WRs / same signal date / same "neutral" tag) with slightly different MTM** — SUCCESSFUL (investigation only, no code changes)
   - Summary: User attached a screenshot of two ^GSPC BASELINEDIVERGENCE ("BASE") Weekly Long cards with identical Symbol/Function/Interval/Direction/BT WR 83.1%/FWD WR 65.3%/Signal Date "Jul 18"/sentiment "neutral", differing only in MTM. Traced the full chain read-only across all three repos:
     1. **Frontend (Vue, `/home/ubuntu/MindwealthUI_Vue` — outside normal repo scope, read only, no edits made)**: `pages/signals.vue` + `components/signals/SignalRankedCards.vue` render one card per record from a single `GET /api/signals/new` → `loadNewSignals()` call (`server/utils/mindwealth-data.ts:438-493`). No polling, no `setInterval`, no double-fetch, and `useFetch` replaces (never appends) the list — ruled out a client-side stale+live merge or append-on-refresh bug. Confirmed there **is no dedup**: `recordsToSignals`/`recordToSignal` (`server/utils/signal-parsers.ts:71-93,164-166`) map every upstream record 1:1 to a card. A `signalKey()` identity helper exists (`utils/signal-detail.ts:4-6`, keyed on symbol+function+signal_date+interval+signal_type, deliberately excluding MTM) but is only used as a Vue `:key`, not for de-duplicating the list before render.
     2. **API (`/home/ubuntu/uiv2/git/MindWealth_UI`)**: `api/services/reports_service.py:353-391` loads the `new_signal.csv` / `all_signal.csv` report rows as-is (`dataframe_to_records`) and `api/services/signal_enrichment_service.py:458-509` only parses the existing `Current Mark to Market and Holding Period` string into `mtm_pct` — it does not recompute MTM and does not collapse duplicate rows. So if the upstream CSV already has two rows for the same signal, the API faithfully returns both.
     3. **Root cause (MindWealth engine, `/home/ubuntu/MindWealth`, read only, no edits made)**: BASELINEDIVERGENCE signal generation (`helper_functions/general_divergence.py`) has **no identity dedup** anywhere in the pipeline before rows reach the report/email builder (`util.py`, `send_email.py`). Best-fit mechanism: the nullification-merge loop in `all_update_for_one_interval` (`helper_functions/general_divergence.py:1044-1053`) does `dflist += new_dflist` — appending the **entire** truncated backtest list (not just the newly-nullified entry) whenever a new entry appears one day before `to_date`. This can re-append an already-present entry from a `df` that ends one bar earlier, so MTM (`Close[-1]`-based, `general_divergence.py:929-937`) comes out slightly different between the two copies of the same entry, while Function/Symbol/Interval/Direction/Signal Date/win-rates all stay identical (win rates are strategy-level, not per-entry). A ready-made fix already exists but is dead code: `get_signal_row_identity()` (`send_email.py:825-832`) builds exactly the right dedup key (Function, Symbol+Signal+Date/Price, Interval+Confirmation, Exit) and would collapse this specific duplicate — but it has **zero call sites** anywhere in the codebase (confirmed via search); neither `get_all_new_signal`/`get_all_outstanding_signal`/`get_all_signal` (`send_email.py:722-843`) nor any CSV-writing step ever calls it.
     4. Also checked and **ruled out**: double stake-list membership (`^GSPC` appears exactly once in `data/stake.csv`, zero times in `observationstake.csv`); active multi-lookback-window param sets (the `lst=[900,500,240,120,60]` loop is commented out, only one 5y lookback runs); the `^DJI`/`^NDX` pairing logic (only adds one counterpart column, doesn't duplicate the base row). The two rows are **not** two genuinely different re-entries in this specific screenshot's case (both show the exact same "Jul 18" signal date) — genuinely different re-entry weeks (e.g. the `2026-07-26` vs `2026-07-19` ^GSPC pair found live in `trade_store/US/2026-07-27_all_signal.csv`) is a separate, legitimate pattern that also exists in the data but is not what's pictured here.
   - Conclusion given to user: bug is in the MindWealth signal-generation pipeline (nullification-merge fan-out with no post-hoc dedup), not the UI. Fix would be either (a) call the existing but unused `get_signal_row_identity()` to dedup `outputdict_list`/report rows before write, or (b) fix the `dflist += new_dflist` fan-out in `general_divergence.py:1044-1053` to append only the new entry. No code changes made — user asked for analysis/cause only.
   - Files touched: none (read-only investigation across `/home/ubuntu/uiv2/git/MindWealth_UI`, `/home/ubuntu/MindWealth`, `/home/ubuntu/MindwealthUI_Vue`).
   - **Follow-up (same day, same conversation):** user asked to fix it. Picked option (a) as the safer wiring — full details logged in `/home/ubuntu/.cursor/global_repo_todos.md` (2026-07-29 #1) per that repo's own tracking rule, since the actual code change is in `/home/ubuntu/MindWealth`, not this repo. Summary: wired `get_signal_row_identity()` into a new `dedupe_signal_and_fig_lists()` helper called once in `send_email.py` right after `get_common_column()`, upstream of all three report generators. 5 new regression tests added and passing (`MindWealth/tests/test_signal_dedup.py`). No `MindWealth_UI` files were touched by this fix; no `chatbot-dev`/`chatbot-prod` migration impact for this repo.

2. **Fix: Conviction page shows two different timestamps (top bar vs SOURCE strip)** — SUCCESSFUL
   - Summary: User reported the Conviction page header (`Jul 24, 04:00 PM EDT` from `GET /api/meta` → trade-store report date) disagreed with the regime strip (`conviction_store live · as of 2026-07-26`). Root cause: `loadConviction()` in `MindwealthUI_Vue/server/utils/mindwealth-data.ts` set `asOf = max(last_daily_update across tickers, overlay score-sheet date)`. The conviction fundamentals cron can refresh `last_daily_update` ahead of the nightly signal batch, bumping the strip date while the global header stayed on the outstanding-signal report date. This regressed after the Jul-20 timezone fix (`GET /api/v1/meta` + market-close ET formatting), which only aligned the top bar — it did not stop the conviction strip from using a separate, newer date source. Fix: set `asOf` from the same canonical site report date as `/api/meta` (`loadMeta().data_updated_at.date`), falling back to overlay date; keep `storeLive` when `last_daily_update >= overlaySignalsDate` without using it as the displayed date.

4. **Run the dev-side universe rollout for the SEC EDGAR PE History Fix** — SUCCESSFUL (data-only change: refreshed `conviction_store/*.json` on dev via existing tooling, no code changes)
   - Summary: Follow-up to entry #3 same date (status check found the fix was deployed but never rolled out to any stored record). User said "go ahead." Ran `.venv/bin/python3 scripts/update_conviction_fundamentals.py --mode full --include-existing-records --pe-history-report` on **dev only** (prod is explicitly off-limits for agent-driven `conviction_store` writes per repo rules — see PE-01b in the TODO section). Because `--include-existing-records` pulls in the entire existing store regardless of any `--tickers` filter, a 3-ticker verification run (`PYPL,AAPL,ABNB`) ended up processing and recalculating **all 193 dev records** in one pass (~8 minutes, network-bound on yfinance + SEC EDGAR calls) — confirmed after the fact and treated as the full rollout rather than re-running it a second time. **Result: zero `fetch_errors` across all 193 records.** P/E-history depth distribution shifted dramatically: 32 equities now carry ≥10 years of real trailing-P/E history (were capped at ≤2y before), including MSFT/ADBE/GS/JPM/MCD/NKE/ORCL/PG/UPS at ~17y (206 monthly points), AAPL 190 points, NVDA 178, PYPL 133 — all matching the live-verified depths from the 2026-07-24 development pass almost exactly. **PYPL's original bug is now fixed on dev**: `pe_hist_percentile` component −3.0 → 0.0, `valuation_tax` −4.0 → −1.0 (the remaining −1.0 is the unrelated EV/Revenue entry-multiple component). Spot-checked the rest of the universe for the same `pe_percentile_20y ∈ {0.0, 100.0}` + non-zero `pe_hist_percentile` bug signature that PYPL/AAPL/ABNB originally showed: **only one ticker still has it — `SONY`** (`pe_percentile_20y=100.0`, `pe_hist_percentile=-3.0`, still just 4 yfinance points). Root-caused: `is_us_ticker("SONY")` returns `True` (bare ticker, no recognized non-US suffix — matches the exact "silently misclassifies as US" caveat flagged when `pe_history_fmp.py` was first built on 2026-07-24), but Sony is a Japanese company filing 20-F, not 10-K/10-Q, so SEC EDGAR correctly finds no usable `EarningsPerShareDiluted`/`Basic` facts and returns `None` — SONY falls through to FMP (unprovisioned key, no-ops) and lands back on yfinance's thin 4-quarter series, reproducing the original bug for this one name. Flagged as a follow-up (exception-list `is_us_ticker()` for known bare-ticker foreign filers, or route SONY through the existing `scripts/set_manual_pe_history.py`) rather than fixed in this pass, since it's a single isolated ticker, not a systemic issue.
   - Files changed: `conviction_store/*.json` (193 dev records refreshed — runtime data, not git-tracked). No source code changed.

### 2026-07-29 (continued — SEC pre-2009 legacy-filing PE-history extension, this session)

8. **Research free alternatives for genuine 20-30y PE history, then build a pre-2009 SEC legacy-filing extractor** — SUCCESSFUL
   - Summary: Direct follow-up to entry #4 above (dev SEC EDGAR XBRL rollout). User was told the "PE percentile (20Y)" gauge would still show blank/N-A for almost every ticker since even the best-covered names (MSFT/ADBE/GS/JPM/MCD/NKE/ORCL/PG/UPS) only reached ~17y from SEC's XBRL API (which only has data from each filer's own XBRL-tagging start, ~2007-2011 — a structural floor, not a bug), and asked to "find a source to get the full history for all tickers, explore the web to find free alternatives, carefully find a solution."
   - **Phase 1 — web research on free alternatives (live-tested, not just doc claims):**
     - **Alpha Vantage `EARNINGS` endpoint** — tested live against the real API (`function=EARNINGS&symbol=IBM`): returns quarterly EPS back to **1996** (30 years) for a mature filer, categorically deeper than SEC EDGAR's XBRL floor. **Ruled out for legal reasons, not technical ones**: read Alpha Vantage's actual Terms of Service, which explicitly excludes any "investment analysis, research" use by "a corporation, firm, partnership, trust or any other association" or anyone "employed or [with] an active affiliation with a ... investment advisor, ... money manager ..." from the free tier — MindWealth is exactly this. Using their free key here would be a real ToS violation, flagged explicitly rather than quietly built on.
     - **Finnhub** — their homepage's "30+ years of fundamentals" claim is real but confirmed **paid-only** ($3,500/mo "All-In-One" tier per their own pricing page); the $0 free tier has no historical-fundamentals depth at all.
     - **stockanalysis.com** — no official API; unofficial Apify scrapers exist, but the underlying site itself caps at 5y free / 10y paid — *less* depth than SEC EDGAR already provides, so scraping it would be a regression.
     - **FMP** — unchanged, still 5y free (already the lowest-priority fallback since 2026-07-24).
     - Presented all findings to the user via `AskQuestion` given the ToS complication and trade-offs (accept the ~15-19y ceiling / build a pre-2009 EDGAR text-filing extractor / pay for a vendor / pursue an Alpha Vantage commercial license). **User chose: invest engineering time in a pre-2009 EDGAR full-text (HTML) EPS extractor** for the tickers already near the 20y bar.
   - **Phase 2 — live filing-structure research before writing any parser** (to avoid shipping a fragile scraper that silently produces wrong numbers into a live investment product): fetched real historical filings directly from `data.sec.gov`/`www.sec.gov` for MSFT and JPM's predecessor CIK (Chase Manhattan) and found **two genuinely structured (not scraped-table-guessing) sources**, neither previously used by this codebase:
     1. **EX-27 "Financial Data Schedule"** — a mandatory, tag-delimited mini-schema (`<EPS-DILUTED>2.63`) filed on every 10-K/10-Q from ~1994 until the SEC eliminated the requirement for periods ending after 2001-06-15. Live-verified MSFT FY1997 `EPS-DILUTED=2.63` matches Microsoft's own contemporaneous press release and investor-relations "financial highlights" page exactly; live-verified the same tag names work identically on Chase Manhattan's FY1997 Article-9 bank-holding-company schedule (`EPS-DILUTED=8.03`) — confirming the format is industry-agnostic.
     2. **"Selected Financial Data" (Item 6) table** — SEC-mandated 5-year comparative EPS table inside every 10-K for that era, bridging the ~2001-2009 dead zone between EX-27's phase-out and each company's XBRL start; one well-chosen filing typically closes the whole gap. Live-verified MSFT's FY2002 10-K returns the exact 5 published FY1998-FY2002 diluted-EPS figures, including correctly skipping the adjacent "before accounting change" adjusted-EPS line that sits right next to the real GAAP line in the same table.
   - **Implementation:**
     - Refactored `pe_history_core.py`: split `compute_pe_history()` into `_rolling_ttm_from_quarterly()` (unchanged rolling-4-quarter-sum logic) + `_pe_from_ttm_series()` (the date-walking/monthly-resample/meta logic), and added `compute_pe_history_with_legacy_annual()` — pre-2009 annual EPS figures are *already* trailing-twelve-months as of their fiscal year end (that's what an annual figure is), so they skip the rolling-sum step and get concatenated directly with the modern rolling-TTM series, restricted to dates strictly before the modern series' earliest point (never overrides or duplicates XBRL-era coverage). `compute_pe_history()` itself is now a thin wrapper — zero behavior change, confirmed by the pre-existing `TestPeHistory` tests still passing unmodified.
     - **Also raised `PE_HISTORY_MAX_STORED_POINTS` from 240 (~20y) to 360 (~30y)** — caught during live validation: the old 240-point cap would have silently truncated the *stored* array (and therefore `pe_percentile_20y`'s ranking window) back down to a 20y rolling window even after successfully extending a ticker's real coverage to 30+ years, so `insufficient_20y` would flip to `False` but the percentile itself would still only ever rank against the trailing 20y — defeating the actual point of the extension. Confirmed `engine._percentile_rank()` uses whatever's in `pe_20y_array` with no additional truncation, so this was the correct, minimal lever (checked no test/consumer hardcodes the old 240 value).
     - New `src/conviction_engine/pe_history_sec_legacy.py`: `parse_ex27_annual_eps()` (extracts `PERIOD-END`/`EPS-DILUTED` with fallback to `EPS-PRIMARY`, rejects non-annual `PERIOD-TYPE`, treats a literal `EPS-DILUTED=0` as "not populated" and falls through to primary — a real pattern found in pre-SFAS-128 [1997] schedules that don't compute a separate fully-diluted figure), `parse_selected_financial_data()` (anchors each table column to a specific fiscal-year-end date using the filing's own known `reportDate` rather than trying to parse the inconsistently-worded "Year Ended ..." header text — more robust), `_list_all_10k_filings()` (paginates SEC's `submissions/CIK{cik}.json` + its `filings.files` continuation pages), `fetch_legacy_annual_eps()` (orchestrates: EX-27 for every pre-2001-06-15 filing found, one Selected-Financial-Data bridge lookup for the newest pre-XBRL filing, capped at 12 filings/ticker to bound worst-case network cost, disk-cached 365 days since these filings never change).
     - Wired into `pe_history_sec.py`'s `fetch_pe_history_sec()`: the legacy extension is **only attempted when the XBRL-only bundle is still `insufficient_20y`** (bounds the extra network cost to genuinely gap tickers, not the whole universe), wrapped in a broad `try/except` so a legacy-parsing failure for one ticker can never break its already-working XBRL bundle, and only kept if it strictly increases `point_count`. Tagged `meta.source = "sec_edgar+legacy"` when it contributes.
   - **Live validation (real network calls, real filings, cross-checked against independent sources — not part of the automated suite):** MSFT 18.08y → **32.08y** (source flips to `sec_edgar+legacy`, span 1994-06-30 to present, `insufficient_20y: False`); JPM 17.06y → **31.57y**; GS 17.07y → **26.67y** (correctly stops at GS's actual 1999 IPO, doesn't fabricate earlier data); PG 17.06y → **32.08y**; NKE 17.14y → **31.16y**. PYPL (2015 IPO, no pre-2009 filings exist) correctly stays unextended. Spot-checked several extracted EX-27 values against independent public sources during debugging (Microsoft's own IR "financial highlights" page, contemporaneous press releases) — exact matches. Confirmed apparent "jumps" across era boundaries (e.g. MSFT 1996's $3.43 vs 1997's $2.63) are the already-documented, expected split-adjustment discontinuity (MSFT's Dec-1996 2-for-1 split), not parsing errors — cross-verified the ratio matches the known split.
   - **Test coverage:** new `tests/test_pe_history_sec_legacy.py` (32 tests, all network mocked, using real-filing-derived fixtures: date/number parsing, SGML document extraction incl. `EX-27.1` suffix handling, EX-27 parsing across Article-5-commercial and Article-9-bank schedules + the zero-diluted-fallback case + quarterly-period rejection, Selected-Financial-Data table parsing incl. skipping the adjusted-EPS line/TOC-only false positives/basic-EPS fallback/HTML-tag stripping, filing-list pagination, and full orchestration incl. caching + partial-failure resilience). Added `TestLegacyExtension` (4 tests) to `tests/test_pe_history_sec.py` covering the gating logic (only attempted when insufficient, never attempted when already sufficient, safely falls back on empty/exception). Added `TestPeHistoryWithLegacyAnnual` (4 tests) to `tests/test_conviction_engine.py` for the core merge function. Full regression suite: **627 passed, 2 skipped, 1 pre-existing unrelated failure** (`test_d6_smoke.py` — same leftover git-conflict-marker syntax error in `/home/ubuntu/MindWealth/testing.py`, a different repo, already documented on 2026-07-24/27) — zero regressions.
   - **Unrelated observation, not investigated further (out of scope for this task):** live-testing hit a case where SEC's `companyconcept` API returned `"units":{"USD/shares":{}}` (an empty object, not a facts array) for PYPL's `EarningsPerShareDiluted`/`Basic` concepts specifically — causing `fetch_pe_history_sec("PYPL", ...)` to return `None` entirely (the pre-existing, already-safe "no facts found" path, unchanged by this work).    - Summary: User said "go ahead" to run the rollout proposed in entry #8. First pass (`update_conviction_fundamentals.py --mode full --include-existing-records --pe-history-report`, 193/193 updated, 0 errors) reported a suspiciously small result: only **5** tickers reached `sec_edgar+legacy` (MSFT/JPM/GS/PG/NKE — exactly the 5 already manually re-fetched during entry #8's live validation a few minutes earlier) and `sufficient_20y_count` only moved 0→5 universe-wide, with the "15-20y" bucket still showing 22 tickers untouched (ADBE/UPS/MCD/ORCL/SBUX/AAPL/BAC/CSCO/CVS/MAR/MU/NVDA/PFE/WMT/etc.) despite entry #8's design explicitly targeting large legacy-eligible names like these.
   - **Root cause (caught by spot-checking the actual stored JSON, not just trusting the summary report):** `fetch_pe_history_sec()`'s on-disk XBRL cache (`{TICKER}_sec.json`, 80-day TTL) returns cached bundles on an early-exit `if cached is not None: return cached` **before** ever reaching the `if bundle["meta"].get("insufficient_20y"):` gate that triggers the new legacy extension. 42 of the ~193 tickers still had a valid, unexpired XBRL cache file from the original 2026-07-24 SEC EDGAR rollout — so the full-recalculation run correctly refreshed their *other* fundamentals but served their *PE history* straight from the pre-legacy-extension cache, silently skipping the brand-new code entirely. Confirmed directly: manually calling `fetch_pe_history_sec("ADBE", ...)` after deleting its stale cache immediately produced `sec_edgar+legacy`, `31.67y` — proving the extension logic itself was correct all along; only the cache layer was hiding it.
   - **Fix:** deleted all 42 stale `conviction_store/pe_history_cache/*_sec.json` files (left the `*_sec_legacy.json` legacy-filing caches untouched — those are correctly 365-day-cached since old filings never change) and reran the exact same `full_recalculation` command. **Corrected result:** 193/193 updated, 0 errors, **18 tickers** now `sec_edgar+legacy` (AAPL 31.83y, ADBE 31.67y, BAC 31.57y, CSCO 31.0y, CVS 31.57y, GS 26.67y, JPM 31.57y, MAR 25.58y, MCD 31.57y, MSFT 32.08y, MU 31.91y, NKE 31.16y, NVDA 27.49y, PFE 31.57y, PG 32.08y, SBUX 29.83y, UPS 26.58y, WMT 31.49y — up from the false 5), `sufficient_20y_count` 0→**18** (was mis-reported as 5 on the poisoned first pass), `insufficient_20y_count` 133→115 (86.5%, down from the pre-legacy-extension 96.2% baseline), "15-20y" bucket shrank 22→9 tickers. `pe_percentile_20y` is now populated (non-null) for all 18 for the first time, e.g. AAPL 100.0, WMT 97.22, NVDA 95.39, JPM 89.72. PYPL remains unextended (`source=yfinance`, `0.57y`) — confirmed still reproducing the SEC-empty-facts quirk flagged in entry #8, unrelated to this fix, `valuation_tax` correctly stays at the already-fixed `-1.0` (not the old `-4.0` bug). Remaining "15-20y" bucket (ORCL 17.16y, AMZN 17.08y, AMD 15.59y, AVGO 8.74y, GOOG 10.08y among others) either predate SEC EDGAR full-text availability insufficiently or genuinely lack qualifying pre-2009 filings (e.g. GOOG's 2004 IPO postdates EX-27's 2001 phase-out entirely) — expected, not a bug.
   - **Caveat for future rollouts, now documented as a hard rule:** any time PE-history *fetch logic* changes (not just data), the on-disk `pe_history_cache/*_sec.json` files must be purged first, or the cache-hit early-return will silently serve stale results computed under the old logic — the batch script has no code-version-awareness, only a data-age TTL. Recorded in `docs/dev_to_prod_migration_todos.md` as a mandatory pre-step for the still-pending prod rollout.
   - Files changed: none (rollout-only; `conviction_store/*.json` + `conviction_store/pe_history_cache/*.json` are gitignored runtime data, not tracked).

10. **Extend PE history to non-US tickers: Canada MJDS/IFRS extension (built + rolled out), SEDAR+ and India NSE/BSE researched** — SUCCESSFUL (Canada); researched-but-not-built (India, by design — see below)
    - Summary: Direct follow-up to entries #4/#8/#9 — with the US-side SEC EDGAR + legacy-filing work done, user asked "I need to find a solution to this for all the tickers, what should I do, give options" for the remaining 86.5%-insufficient dev universe. Data-driven categorization of the 115 still-insufficient equities found: ~67 on local foreign exchanges (`.TO` Canada ×30, `.NZ` NZ ×17, `.NS` India ×14, `.KS`/`.HK`/`.SI`/`.F` ×6), ~16 bare-ticker foreign filers (ADRs like BABA/TSM/NVO/WPM/NU), and the rest genuine US IPOs/unexplained-gap tickers. User chose (via `AskQuestion`) to pursue: (a) research per-country free sources for the ~67 local-exchange tickers, then specifically (b) build the Canada MJDS extension, (c) build an India BSE fetcher, (d) research SEDAR+.
    - **Canada — real, shippable finding, built and rolled out:** Live-verified that 6 major TSX names (TD, RY, BNS, CNQ, TRI, BN) are dual-listed on a US exchange and file **40-F** annual reports (+ **6-K** interim updates) with the SEC under the MJDS regime — a form type and `ifrs-full` XBRL taxonomy (`BasicEarningsLossPerShare`/`DilutedEarningsLossPerShare`) the existing `pe_history_sec.py` never checked (it only looked at `us-gaap` + 10-K/10-Q). Also found this data is **currency-denominated per filer**: TD/RY/BNS/CNQ report in CAD (matches their `.TO`, CAD-priced TSX listing exactly — no FX needed); TRI/BN report in USD only (would need FX to pair with a CAD-priced `.TO` quote — **not** implemented, deliberately deferred, see below). Shipped a manually-verified allowlist (`FOREIGN_PRIVATE_ISSUER_ALIASES` in `pe_history_sec.py`) covering the 4 currency-safe names (TD.TO, RY.TO, BNS.TO, CNQ.TO) — deliberately **not** a blanket "any foreign bare ticker" rule, since SEC's ticker→CIK map has real collisions with unrelated shell companies for some Canadian tickers (bare "NA" and "SJ" resolve to unrelated OTC issuers, not National Bank of Canada / Stella-Jones — discovered and avoided during research, not shipped).
      - TD/RY/BNS have genuine quarter-duration (~80-100 day) facts filed via 6-K, so they reuse the existing Q4-plug quarterly-reconstruction path unchanged (just with the new taxonomy/currency/form parameters). CNQ has **zero** quarter-duration facts for this concept (only annual 40-F totals) — added `_build_annual_only_eps_series()` and routed it through the already-existing `compute_pe_history_with_legacy_annual()` (passing an empty quarterly series) rather than writing new merge logic, since that function already treats annual points as already-TTM.
      - Currency-mismatch is a hard `None` (never a wrong ratio): `_fetch_concept_facts()` now takes a `currency` parameter and looks up e.g. `"CAD/shares"` specifically; if that unit isn't present, the fetch fails cleanly rather than silently pairing e.g. USD EPS with a CAD price.
      - Live-validated (real network calls, not mocked) against the actual `data.sec.gov` API: TD.TO/RY.TO/BNS.TO 0.48y→**7.74y**, CNQ.TO 0.57y→**8.57y** — all `pe_history_meta.source="sec_edgar_40f"`. Still `insufficient_20y=True` for all 4 (SEC's inline-XBRL mandate for MJDS filers only started ~2016, so ~8-10y is close to the real ceiling here, same structural floor pattern as the original ~2009 US XBRL floor) — but a large, real improvement over the ~0.3-0.8y thin-yfinance baseline they had before.
      - **Rolled out to dev**: ran `update_conviction_fundamentals.py --mode full --tickers "TD.TO,RY.TO,BNS.TO,CNQ.TO" --include-existing-records --pe-history-report` (note: `--include-existing-records` re-processes the *whole* store regardless of `--tickers`, not just the 4 named — confirmed via the report's before/after bucket counts matching exactly the expected ±4 shift with zero collateral changes elsewhere, e.g. AAPL/MSFT/PYPL spot-checked unchanged). Confirmed on-disk: all 4 tickers now `source=sec_edgar_40f` with the expected years/valuation_tax values.
      - **Test coverage:** new `TestForeignPrivateIssuerAlias` (8 tests) + `TestBuildAnnualOnlyEpsSeries` (3 tests) in `tests/test_pe_history_sec.py`, all network-mocked — covering the quarterly path, the annual-only path, diluted→basic IFRS concept fallback, the currency-mismatch-returns-None guard, cache round-trip, and a regression guard confirming a non-aliased `.TO` ticker (SHOP.TO) still makes zero network calls exactly as before this feature existed. Also fixed a `pd.concat` `FutureWarning` in `compute_pe_history_with_legacy_annual()` surfaced during live validation (filter empty series before concat). Full regression suite: 637 passed, 2 skipped, 1 pre-existing unrelated failure (`test_d6_smoke.py`, passes in isolation — confirmed zero connection to conviction_engine/pe_history code, a test-ordering flake).
      - **Deliberately deferred, not built this pass:** (1) TRI.TO/BN.TO — real IFRS USD-denominated EPS data exists on SEC but needs an FX (CAD/USD) conversion step that doesn't exist anywhere in this codebase yet; safer to ship nothing than a silently-wrong ratio. (2) The other ~24 `.TO` tickers in the dev universe (SJ.TO, NA.TO, ATD.TO, smaller-caps, etc.) are Canada-exchange-only with **no** US SEC presence at all — this extension structurally cannot help them.
    - **SEDAR+ (Canada's own filing database) — researched, concluded not worth building on:** confirmed via CSA's own FAQ + multiple independent sources that SEDAR+ **does not accept or provide XBRL filings at all** (PDF-only) and has **no official public developer API** (unofficial wrappers exist but only return filing *metadata* + PDF download URLs, never parsed financial figures). Building on this would mean bespoke per-issuer PDF financial-statement parsing for ~24 idiosyncratic filers with no structured schedule to lean on (materially harder than the SEC EX-27/Selected-Financial-Data pre-2009 extractor, which had a genuine mini-schema to parse) — recommended against pursuing this further; the remaining Canada-only names are better served by the existing manual-entry track (PE-03) than new engineering here.
    - **India NSE/BSE — researched, concluded the "clean official API" premise was wrong, flagged back rather than built blind:** the `financeindia` library that looked promising turned out to target **NSE**, not BSE, and further digging found: (1) NSE's real quarterly/annual XBRL results exist but sit behind **Akamai bot-protection** requiring a manually-copied browser session cookie (the same fragile, ToS-adjacent, needs-periodic-manual-refresh risk class that already ruled out the Macrotrends approach earlier in this project — not a clean key-based API like SEC's) — confirmed via the `nse-xbrl` library's own README, which explicitly documents the copy-cookie-from-DevTools workflow and warns "NSE can change its bot-protection or response formats at any time, which may break [this] without notice." (2) Even if that bot-protection is bypassed, the current "Integrated Filing" XBRL format only exists **from 2024 onward** — so it would only add ~2 years of depth, not solve the 20-year problem on its own. (3) BSE's own structured corporate-data product is exclusively paid-licensed through Deutsche Börse; BSE's free public endpoints are Announcements/metadata only, not financial-figures XBRL. Deeper historical depth for India would require either the same cookie-bypass risk for pre-2024 "Financial Results" XBRL (unconfirmed if that's even less protected) or parsing raw annual-report PDFs from `nsearchives.nseindia.com` (static files, going back to ~2011+ for large filers like IOC) — i.e. a new PDF-parsing project, not a clean API integration. **Did not write speculative fetcher code against a bot-protected, format-limited source without the user's explicit go-ahead given this materially different risk/effort profile than what was originally scoped** — reported back for a decision instead (see TODO section).
    - Files changed: `src/conviction_engine/pe_history_sec.py` (`FOREIGN_PRIVATE_ISSUER_ALIASES`, `_fetch_foreign_private_issuer()`, `_build_annual_only_eps_series()`, currency-aware `_fetch_concept_facts()`), `src/conviction_engine/pe_history_core.py` (FutureWarning fix in `compute_pe_history_with_legacy_annual()`), `src/conviction_engine/fundamentals_enriched.py` (call-site gate updated to allow the alias list past the `is_us_ticker()` check), `tests/test_pe_history_sec.py` (+11 tests), `conviction_store/{TD,RY,BNS,CNQ}.TO.json` + full store refresh (gitignored runtime data, not tracked).

### 2026-07-24

1. **Investigate: "Valuation Tax / P/E percentile wrong for all assets" complaint (Divyanshu/Rohit chat, PYPL example)** — SUCCESSFUL (investigation only, no code changes)
   - Summary: User pasted a chat where a reporting manager tells Divyanshu "all macro engine outputs are wrong because P/E percentile has been set at [near] 0 for all, leading to too-high Valuation Tax" and that PYPL should have 0 valuation tax. Confirmed: (1) there is no separate "macro engine" valuation-tax logic — this is the **Conviction Engine** (`src/conviction_engine/`), Valuation Tax = `scoring.calculate_valuation_tax*`, PE percentile = `engine._percentile_rank` + `pe_percentile_20y`. (2) **Bug is real and still live on production** (`chatbot-prod`, HEAD `0d7748557` from 2026-07-20): PYPL's prod record shows `pe_percentile_20y=83.33` (ranked off only **8 sparse points spanning 0.0–0.56 years** of yfinance EPS history, not 20y), which trips the `>=70` tier → `pe_hist_percentile=-2.0` → total `valuation_tax=-3.0`. Universe-wide on prod: 35/132 equities show `pe_percentile_20y=0.0` and 27/132 show `100.0` — both extremes are artifacts of ranking against a handful of noisy recent points, not genuine 20-year percentiles; 34 records take the full `-3.0` PE tax hit from this. (3) **A fix already exists on `chatbot-dev`** (uncommitted working-tree changes, per `instruction_docs/ssi_and_conviction_updates/FUNDAMENTAL_AGENT_UPDATE_JULY_2026_STATUS.md`, completed 2026-07-22, verified on PYPL: PE tax −2→**0**) — `engine.py` now nulls `pe_percentile_20y` whenever `pe_history_meta.insufficient_20y` is true, so `pe_hist_percentile` stays neutral (0.0) instead of taxing off a garbage rank. Confirmed dev PYPL today: `pe_percentile_20y=null`, `pe_hist_percentile=0.0`, `valuation_tax=-1.0` (from the unrelated EV/Revenue `entry_multiple` component only). **Root cause of "still wrong" complaint: the dev fix was never committed to git and never merged/deployed to `chatbot-prod`** — `git status` shows `engine.py`/`scoring.py` still as uncommitted modifications on `chatbot-dev`, and this was already flagged `[PENDING]` in `dev_to_prod_migration_todos.md` under "2026-07-22 — Fundamental Agent Update (conviction engine)". (4) **New finding from this pass:** the dev fix is arguably over-broad — `PE_HISTORY_TARGET_YEARS=20` means almost no yfinance-sourced ticker ever qualifies, so `pe_percentile_20y` is now `None` for **129/133 (97%)** of the dev universe and the PE-percentile tax component fires for **only 1 ticker (SONY)** universe-wide. This fixes the false-positive (PYPL) but effectively disables the entire PE-percentile tax mechanism for nearly everyone — worth a product decision on whether a lower minimum-history bar (e.g. ≥3–5y / N quarters) should still drive a percentile tax instead of full neutrality. Separately, the EV/Revenue `entry_multiple` component (unaffected by this bug) still taxes 88/133 dev-universe equities (45 at −5, 20 at −7, etc.) — if "everything is wrong" refers to overall `valuation_tax` magnitude broadly (not just the PE component), that lever should be reviewed too since it wasn't in scope of the July-22 fix.
   - Files changed: none (read-only investigation; updated `docs/dev_to_prod_migration_todos.md` note only)

2. **Super Sentiment dashboard: layer-score display bug (composite still "looked" mismatched after the Q1 fix)** — SUCCESSFUL
   - Summary: User flagged the composite-vs-layers discrepancy again after the 2026-07-21 superindex fix, screenshot showed composite +0.2, Layer 1 +0.6, Layer 2 +0.0, Layer 3 +0.0. Root cause was **not** a calculation bug this time — full-precision live values (layer1=0.5871, layer2=0.0047, layer3=-0.0318, weights 40/35/25) sum to exactly 0.2285, matching `ssi_level` — but two dashboard display bugs made it look wrong: (1) `roundLayerScore()`/`formatLayerScore()` in the Nuxt UI (`MindwealthUI_Vue`) rounded layer scores to only 1 decimal, too coarse to verify the weighted average by eye; (2) rounding layer3's -0.0318 to 1dp produced JS negative zero (`-0`), and `score >= 0 ? '+' : ''` is `true` for `-0` in JS, so a genuinely negative layer score displayed as `+0.0` instead of a minus sign. Fixed: bumped layer-score and composite-score display to 2 decimals, and rewrote both sign checks to derive the sign from the already-rounded value with `Math.abs()` instead of comparing the raw score directly (immune to the `-0` trap). Now shows composite +0.23, Layer 1 +0.59, Layer 2 +0.00, Layer 3 -0.03 — checkable by hand (0.4×0.59 + 0.35×0.00 + 0.25×(-0.03) = 0.2285 ≈ 0.23) and correctly signed. Verified the exact formatting logic against the live API numbers with a standalone Node script; rebuilt and restarted `mindwealth-ui-dev`.
   - Files changed: `MindwealthUI_Vue/server/utils/sentiment-mapper.ts`, `MindwealthUI_Vue/pages/sentiment.vue`
